# Flink levels for Lomax

This mod plays levels from *The Misadventures of Flink* (Mega-CD, 1994) in *Lomax* (PC, 1997). Both games are by the same developers. Two parts do the work:

- **`FLINK.EXE`** converts Flink's levels into Lomax world files (`.WLD`) and extracts its music and sound effects, all from the Flink disc image. Both games use the same level editor's format, with different byte order, packing, pixel depth and palettes. The collision bits are the same.
- **`FLINK.DLL`** makes the game play them in place of Lomax's levels: Flink's 31 levels become levels 1–31, and the maps of each become its sub-levels.

The mod is independent of Lomax+. FLINK.DLL patches the retail `LOMAX.EXE` itself, from `DllMain`, and uses no Lomax+ code, hooks or settings. Anything that loads it into the game before the game's code runs will do. The current host is Lomax+, which lists each folder `MODS\<name>` holding `<name>.DLL` and `<name>.INI` under Properties > Extra > Load Mod and loads the chosen one's DLL at the next start; a standalone launcher or a proxy DLL could do the same.

What is converted: graphics, palette, layout, collision, most platforms, the Jumppads, the foreground Trees, the lianas and the monkeys, the Knols, the Spikes, 10-3's drop balls, TREE's Falling apples, rolling apples (the Apple (big) and App2) and Butterflies, 3-2's Snake, the first enemies (the Forest Dummy, the City dummy, the Mountain dummy with its shield, META's flying Meta dummy, its Storm Cloud blowing wind and its Blitz dude striking lightning from his cloud, its Circling suckers closing in on Lomax in a ring, the Yeti, the Buzzard, 21-1's stampede of Razorbacks, the flying Cyclin' Dude with his Chopper Bike, which Lomax can ride, and the Little Dudes he drops, the Firemonster, the Ingredient Holder, the Thief, the Visje and the Statue), the treasure chests, the stones, and the hidden objects that Flink's Generators make appear. Lomax picks things up and throws them as Flink does, with the Ability button (see Grabbing and throwing). Flink's other enemies, items and spells are not, so some sections can't be crossed yet.

## Files

| File | What it is |
|---|---|
| `dll\` | FLINK.DLL's source, one file per feature (`main.c` lists them; `game.h` holds Lomax's addresses and actor layout) |
| `extractor\` | `FLINK.EXE`'s source: it makes the mod's data from the Flink disc image, the levels included (see Extractor) |
| `build.bat` | Builds `build\FLINK.DLL` from `dll\`; `build.bat -extractor` builds only `build\FLINK.EXE` from `extractor\` |
| `flink.ini` | The mod's description for Lomax+ (installed as `MODS\FLINK\FLINK.INI`): `[Lomax]` `Name`, `Author`, `Description`, and `Files`, the files Lomax+ checks before loading the mod |

## Installing

1. Build FLINK.DLL (`build.bat`; it needs MSYS2 with mingw32, the same toolchain as Lomax+).
2. Put `FLINK.DLL` in `MODS\FLINK\` in the game folder, and `flink.ini` as `MODS\FLINK\FLINK.INI`.
3. Build `FLINK.EXE` (`build.bat -extractor`), put it and the Flink disc image (`.cue` and `.bin`) in `MODS\FLINK\` too and run it: it writes `WORLDS\`, `MUSIC\` and `SFX\` there (see Extractor).
4. The image can be removed afterwards.

Everything of the mod is in `MODS\FLINK\`; the game's own `WORLDS`, `MUSIC` and `SFX` folders are untouched:

```
MODS\FLINK\FLINK.DLL, FLINK.INI
MODS\FLINK\WORLDS\LEVELS.INI, L01.WLD ... L62.WLD, MAP.WLD ... MAP3.WLD (the world map), TITLE.WLD (the title screen),
                   SCENE0.WLD ... SCENE7.WLD (the intro and the ending)
MODS\FLINK\MUSIC\TRACK102.WAV ... TRACK118.WAV
MODS\FLINK\SFX\E00.WAV ... E46.WAV
```
5. Start the game with `LOADER.EXE`, choose Flink in File > Properties > Extra > Load Mod, restart the game and start a new game.

To turn the mod off, choose None there (or remove the `MODS\FLINK` folder). If a file `FLINK.INI`'s `Files` lists is missing, Lomax+ says so at the start and runs the game without the mod.

The mod has no settings file (`FLINK.INI` only describes it to Lomax+). Its options are constants:
- the levels are always `MODS\FLINK\WORLDS` (`LEVELS_FOLDER`, flink.h);
- Flink's water is `SWIM_MODE` (flink.h): 0 = Lomax drowns, 1 = he floats and never drowns, 2 (the default) = he walks under water (see Water);
- Lomax's speed, jump height and water values are defines at the top of `dll\move.c` (see How FLINK.DLL works, Movement);
- `FLINK.LOG` next to the DLL is written while Lomax+'s log switch is on: the DWORD `HKCU\Software\Psygnosis\Lomax\Extra\Log` (Properties > Extra), read by FLINK.DLL at load (`log_init`, util.c). The DLL only reads the value, so it still needs nothing from the loader: with no value there is no log.

## Extractor

`FLINK.EXE` (`extractor\`, built by `build.bat -extractor` into `build\`) makes the mod's data from the Flink disc image. It is plain C, 32-bit, for Windows XP and later, with no dependencies. It sits in `MODS\FLINK\` next to `FLINK.DLL`, takes no parameters and writes only into its own folder, which is where the mod reads its data; the game folder is two levels up (`..\..`), where the level conversion reads Lomax's own worlds (`WORLDS\MEDIEVAL.WLD`, `MAIN.WLD`, `MAP.WLD`, `TITLE.WLD`) without writing to them:

1. It looks for a `.cue` in its own folder, then in each folder one level down, as Lomax+'s `LOADER.EXE` looks for the Lomax disc. The first `.cue` whose data track holds `FLINK.PRG` is Flink's, so a Lomax disc image beside it is passed over (`disc_open`, disc.c).
2. It reads `FLINK.PRG` from the data track's ISO 9660 file system, in any folder: raw `MODE1/2352` (user data at offset 16), `MODE2/2352` Form 1 (24), `MODE2/2336` (8) or `MODE1/2048` (0). Only the European disc's `FLINK.PRG` is accepted (334180 bytes, CRC32 `7B35BDA7`: `PRG_SIZE`, `PRG_CRC` in main.c), since the addresses the extraction reads are that file's.
3. It writes into its own folder `SFX\E00.WAV` … `E46.WAV` (see Sound effects), `WORLDS\` (below) and `MUSIC\TRACK102.WAV` … `TRACK118.WAV` (see Music; a track already there is kept, the rest is rewritten each run). Each file is written as `NAME.tmp` and renamed when complete, so an interrupted run leaves no half-written file.
4. A missing image, a missing `.bin`, another disc's `FLINK.PRG` or a missing game world is reported in its window. Started from Explorer, it waits for a key before closing.

The whole run takes a few seconds, the music's copy most of it.

## Converting the levels

The level converter (`extractor\convert.c` and the files beside it) reads Flink's world files (`FOREST.WLD`, `VILLAGE.WLD`, `MOUNTAIN.WLD`, `TREE.WLD`, `META.WLD`, `MAIN.WLD`, `INTRO.WLD`, `EXTRO.WLD`) and `FLINK.PRG` from the disc image, and from the game folder Lomax's `WORLDS\MEDIEVAL.WLD` (the template: the object types Start, Finish, Exit, Revive, Music and ScrollMap, their sprites and the sprite palettes 1–9), `WORLDS\MAIN.WLD` (the HUD's digits), `WORLDS\MAP.WLD` (the map screen's objects) and `WORLDS\TITLE.WLD` (the title's objects). It writes into `WORLDS\`:
- `L01.WLD` … `L62.WLD`: every map of Flink's 31 levels and their side rooms (62 files, about 13 MB), named by Flink's own level number (`LevelSequenceTable`; level 1 is FOREST map 19);
- `MAP.WLD` … `MAP3.WLD`: Flink's world map (3.4 MB each, mostly the waving clouds; see World map);
- `TITLE.WLD`: its title screen (see Title screen);
- `SCENE0.WLD` … `SCENE7.WLD`: its intro and ending (see Intro and ending);
- `LEVELS.INI`, which lists each level's maps (see Levels), the hint texts, arrivals, secret levels, areas, music, the world map's, title's and scenes' settings.

Each step prints a line: per map its size, cells, tiles, start and end, platforms, enemies and objects.

Flink's world files are packed with the Amiga XPK library's NUKE packer: `xpk.c` is Flink's own decoder (Xpk_Unpack 0x359F8, Nuke_DecodeChunk 0x35A36, Nuke_DecodeCore 0x35AC4 and its table at 0x35BAE) written in C step for step. The output depends on the order in which cells, tiles and sprite banks are first met (an insertion-ordered hash map, `hmap.c`, keeps it), on floored division (`fdiv`, `fmodp`), on halves rounded to even (`rint`) and on double arithmetic rounding as IEEE doubles: the 32-bit build uses the x87 unit (no SSE2, for the processors Windows XP runs on), set to 53-bit precision at the start (`_controlfp(_PC_53)`), with `-fexcess-precision=standard` (build.bat) so that each float step of the intro's inpainting rounds to float.

## Levels

Flink's world map (MAIN.WLD map 3) has 32 points. One is where the game begins; each of the other 31 starts a level at a map of `LevelSequenceTable`. A level is a chain of maps:
- An `Exit point` leads to another map (its parameters: the map's table index and the revive point to arrive at).
- A `Warp` ends the level and goes back to the world map.

The converter follows each chain from its first map, taking the exit that leads to a new map. A map with neither a forward exit nor a Warp is a boss arena and ends its level.

Doors (exits marked "look up") off a map that also has a Warp lead to 4 side rooms: FOREST maps 9 and 20, VILLAGE map 12 and MOUNTAIN map 13 (`side_rooms`). Each side room is played as an extra sub-level at the end of its level, after the main path (see Doors):
- level 3: 3-4 (FOREST map 9) and 3-5 (FOREST map 20, which ends the level at its own Warp);
- level 13: 13-2 (VILLAGE map 12, whose door leads back further along 13-1);
- level 22: 22-2 (MOUNTAIN map 13).

Level 3 then has 5 sub-levels, as many as Lomax's level table allows.

| Levels | Flink world | Maps per level (+ side rooms) |
|---|---|---|
| 1–9 | FOREST | 2, 2, 3 + 2, 2, 3, 2, 1, 2 (boss), 2 |
| 10–17 | VILLAGE | 4, 2, 1, 1 + 1, 2, 2, 2 (boss), 1 |
| 18–24 | MOUNTAIN | 3, 3, 1, 1, 1 + 1, 2, 2 (boss) |
| 25–28 | TREE | 2, 1, 2, 1 (boss) |
| 29–31 | META | 1, 2, 2 (boss) |

The levels are in the order of Flink's world-map path (`flink_main_levels`): from the start point, each point opens one new neighbour (its UP/DOWN/LEFT/RIGHT parameters; a negative one is a path opened later, such as a secret one). The levels on that path take, in path order, the places they would have in `LevelSequenceTable` order; the others (the secret levels 7 and 17, and the points reached only through paths opened later: MOUNTAIN maps 14–16, TREE, META) keep their table order. The two orders differ only at levels 6 and 8: the path goes from level 5 to FOREST map 22 (6-1) and then to the boss's FOREST maps 16–17 (8-1, 8-2), whose table entries come first. Flink's own world map lets you pick among open paths; Lomax plays its levels in a row, except the two secret levels (see Secret levels).

In each map:
- **Start:** a Start trapdoor over Flink's start point, 80 px above the floor as in Lomax's own levels (they use 72–98 px), or lower under a low ceiling. Flink places its start point on the floor.
- **Checkpoints:** a Lomax `Revive` (checkpoint, object 3) at each of Flink's other revive points, except the entry points (see Arrivals). Flink's are unseen and far more frequent than Lomax's, so in Flink's levels FLINK.DLL hides them: no post is drawn, no sound plays, and a checkpoint fires when Lomax's hitbox reaches its x at any height (Lomax's own needs its point inside his hitbox), while it is spawned, that is while it is about on screen. It saves the same restart point as Lomax's (the checkpoint's x and y, the plane and the camera's orbit offset) and is then removed (`checkpoint_update`, which replaces `Checkpoint_Update` 0x4277A0 and `Checkpoint_Draw` 0x4278C0).
- **Next sub-level:** a Lomax `Exit` at each of Flink's exits to the next map. Walking into it ends the sub-level; Lomax walks on and the screen fades, as in Lomax. While he walks off (`g_bLevelExitReached` set), Player_Update skips its ground search (0x410C4C), which suited Lomax's exits at the flat right end of a map; past Flink's exits the ground can slope or step down, and in 10-1 he ran on in the air. `exit_walk_before` (exits.c, from `player_tick`) looks up the ground under him before each Player_Update (`Plane_FindGroundY` on the current plane, his x and y, 4 cells, as the skipped call) and sets `g_PlayerGroundY` (0x52C558), so he follows the ground. The next map then fades in with Lomax standing at the point that exit leads to, as in Flink (see Arrivals). Some maps have two exits to the same next map, an upper and a lower route (FOREST map 5), and they arrive at different points. Exits that are doors are entered with Up instead (see Doors).
- **End of the level:** a Finish trapdoor over the Warp, 80 px above the floor (less under a low ceiling or the map's top, keeping 52 px over it for the trapdoor's sprite, `TRAPDOOR_SMALL`; a Start keeps 72, room for Lomax to drop out: 21-1's Finish, under the map's top, hung only 40 px over the floor with the Start's rule; 10-4, 22-1 and 25-2, 26-1 were raised too); Lomax enters it with Up, as in his own levels. Boss arenas get it at the floor nearest the map's right end, except 8-2, 16-2, 28-1 and 31-2, whose bosses are converted: there it waits in the middle of the map and appears once the boss is defeated (see Enemies, Boss; 16-2's in the middle of the screen, the map having scrolled on; 28-1's in the middle of Flink's last screen, x 480, where the Donkey monkey's fight is). A main-path map that leads on and also has a Warp gets both its Exits and a Finish over the Warp (14-1: see Secret levels).

### Arrivals

Flink's `Exit point` (0x2AF7A) has the parameters `level` and `entry`. It loads that level with that entry number (0x8318). There, the revive point that is spawned at once and whose `entry` parameter matches (0 counts as 1) puts Flink on itself (0x2B124) and removes itself (0x32CBA). Flink just appears there, standing, and restarts there after losing a life. These entry points are not checkpoints. A level's first map starts at entry 1.

Lomax's next sub-level instead has Lomax walk in from x = −100 at the height of the floor under the Start trapdoor, with the input locked, until he passes the trapdoor's x (`Trapdoor_Init` 0x42A820, `Trapdoor_Update` 0x42AD80). Player_Update doesn't follow the ground during the walk-in (0x410C31). In a Flink map, whose floor at the left is rarely at that height, he walked in the air or under the floor.

So the converter writes, for every Exit, the map it leads to and the arrival point, in `LEVELS.INI`'s `[Arrivals]` (`arrival_points`):

```
L03.WLD 1431 192=L04.WLD 30 256
```

That is the Exit's placement (map file, x, y), then the next map's entry point (map file, x, its floor line). FLINK.DLL looks the Exit up when it fires (`exit_update`). After the next map loads, the Start trapdoor's spawn (`trapdoor_spawn`, object 0) runs `Trapdoor_Init`, which sets up the walk-in, and then replaces it:
- the restart point, Lomax and his floor go to the arrival point (snapped to Lomax's floor there with `Plane_FindGroundY`);
- the camera goes where `Trapdoor_Init` puts it for that point;
- the walk-in is cleared: `g_bLevelExitReached`, `g_bLevelEnding` (which `Actors_UpdateAll` copies into the input lock every tick), the input lock and the trapdoor's +0xA8.

The screen fades in as before, and the trapdoor stays hidden. An Exit with no arrival leaves Lomax on the floor under the Start trapdoor.

There is no "Let's go" after an exit or a door, as in Flink, which shows nothing on a change of map. Trapdoor_Init sets the trapdoor's +0x90 to 10 on the exit path, and Trapdoor_Update plays sound 26 (`LETSGO`) and shows `Insert_Show(1)` when it runs out; `trapdoor_spawn` sets it to -1, as Trapdoor_Update leaves it once done. The banner still comes at the level's start and after a lost life.

### Doors

An `Exit point` with "look up" checked is a door. Flink enters it with Up (0x2AFDC): when he is within 16 px of its x, with his feet 0–80 px above its y, the map loads 10 frames later. A door can lead to any map of the level:
- to the next map on the main path, as for 12 of the main-path transitions (5-1, 5-2, 9-1, 10-2, 10-3, 15-1, 18-1, 18-2, 19-1, 19-2, 23-1 and 24-1);
- into a side room and back;
- to another spot on the same map: FOREST map 10 (3-3) and VILLAGE map 8 (11-1) each have a pair of doors leading to each other.

The converter makes a Lomax `Exit` of every Exit point that leads to a map of its level, and marks doors in `[Arrivals]` with ` door`:

```
L08.WLD 604 560=L08.WLD 1109 96 door
```

In FLINK.DLL (`exit_update`):
- A door's Exit draws nothing: Flink's tiles draw the door, and Lomax's Exit sign belongs only to the exits Lomax walks out of.
- A door fires with Up, within the same range as Flink's, with Lomax on the ground and the input not locked. Up must also have been released since the map loaded. Arriving next to the door that leads back (as in 3-3) with Up still held doesn't take that door at once.
- Lomax then stops (the input lock comes from `g_bLevelEnding`), walks to the door's centre (the Exit's x, Flink's exit point, which is the middle of the doorway: `WALK` at 1.5 px a tick, facing it, at most 16 px) and there walks into the door with the animation of Lomax's own plane doors and bridges, `IN  ` (his back turned), shrinking from 100% to 75% with his feet on the ground (Flink only waits). As he shrinks he is also drawn up to 3 px higher (`WALK_LIFT`, `door_walk_lift`, following the scale: 0 at the turn, 3 px when the screen is black), for a slight sense of depth. The fade-out starts 10 ticks after he turns to the door, and he reaches 75% as the screen is black (74 ticks). Lomax's `PlaneDoor_Update` (0x432D30) also lifts him and draws him toward the plane behind, an arc; FLINK.DLL only borrows its animation and `g_PlaneTransitionScale` (0x52C568): while that is not 0, Player_Update leaves his animation alone (0x411480) and he can't be hit. The player's draw call (`player_draw`, groundtype.c) clears it for the call and passes the scale to `Actor_Draw` instead, whose plain path scales the sprite around his x, y, his feet (`walk_start`, `walk_tick`, `door_walk_scale` in exits.c). The Start trapdoor's spawn on the next map clears state 0x10 and sets `STAY` (`walk_end`).
- `g_bLevelExitReached` is set only as the fade-out ends (`g_FadeStep` −2 or −1; `Game_UpdateFade` 0x40F530 calls Level_Start at 0). While it is set, Player_Update walks Lomax on, which a door shouldn't do.
- Any Exit then sets `g_CurSubLevel` to its target's sub-level − 1, because Level_Start adds 1 before loading. The target can be an earlier sub-level, a side room or the same map.

The arrival then works as for any exit. Losing a life in a side room restarts Lomax at the point where he arrived. A Finish trapdoor ends the level from any sub-level, so 3-5's Warp completes level 3.

### Secret levels

Flink's world map has hidden paths. A `Warp` with checkbox 1 ends its level and opens them (0x2AEAA sets 0x827B when Flink enters it): on the world map, each `Map ` point's neighbour parameters (UP, DOWN, LEFT, RIGHT: params 2–5) that are negative and whose checkbox (bits 2–5) is set become a path to point −param (0x2D420). Two Warps have it, and each opens a small room full of chests:
- 3-5's Warp (FOREST map 20, a side room of level 3) opens point 29: level 7 (FOREST map 21);
- 14-2's Warp (VILLAGE map 20) opens point 32: level 17 (VILLAGE map 21, the same room in VILLAGE's tiles).

The converter finds them (`secret_levels`) and writes them in `LEVELS.INI`:

```
[Secret]
7=L16.WLD
17=L32.WLD
```

Level 7 comes only after level 3 is finished at L16.WLD's Warp, and after level 7 comes level 4. The main flow skips the secret levels:
- 3-5 → 7-1 → 4-1; 3-3's own Warp → 4-1;
- 8-2 → 6-1 (skipping 7-1);
- 14-2 → 17-1 → 15-1; 14-1's Warp → 15-1;
- 16-2 → 25-1 (skipping 17-1; see Play order).

14-1 (VILLAGE map 13) has both an exit to 14-2 (the secret route) and a Warp (the normal way out of level 14). A main-path map that leads on also gets a Finish trapdoor over its Warp (`convert`'s `warp`; 14-1 is the only one).

In FLINK.DLL (`levels.c`): the level-complete banner (`Insert_Show` 4 or 6) counts `g_LastRealSection` up, and the map screen then leaves for that section (`Map_WorldMap_Update`). `setup_next_level`, on its way to the map after a completed level (`g_bGoToMap`, with `g_LastRealSection` = the level just played + 1; a lost life or the time-out goes to the map without counting it up), sets it to the level that comes next (`next_level`), from the level and the sub-level where the Finish was taken.

The secret levels play as Lomax's own bonus level:
- **In:** the Finish that opens one goes straight into it, without the map: the level-complete banner, then the level. `setup_next_level` clears `g_bGoToMap` and sets `g_CurSection`, as `Game_SetupNextLevel` itself does for the bonus trapdoor.
- **Start:** Lomax stands on the floor under the Start trapdoor, with no drop out of it and no "Let's go". `secret_start` in `exits.c`, after `Trapdoor_Init`'s first-start path (taken while `g_RestartX` < 0), puts the trapdoor in the state `Trapdoor_Update` leaves it in after the landing and the banner (+0x80 −200, +0x90 −1, closed `ZWEI`).
- **A death** leaves the level without losing a life, as a fall off the bonus level's bottom does (0x4101D3: `g_bGoToMap`, `g_bSectionChangePending`, a fade-out with the music). Player_Update starts `g_PlayerDeathTimer` at 300 and takes the life at 250 (0x4102AF), or, drowned, takes it at once (0x410160). `secret_after` (`levels.c`, from `player_tick` after Player_Update) leaves on either and puts the life back every tick until the next section loads. The map then leaves for the level after it, as after the Finish.
- **Out:** the Finish goes to the map, as the bonus level's end does, and then on to the level after it.

### Play order

The levels don't all come in number order: `DETOURS` in `levels.c` (FLINK.DLL, not the converter) sends finishing level 16 to 25, 28 to 18, 22 to 29 and 31 to 23, and after 24 the game ends (Flink's ending, then its intro and the title; see Intro and ending). So the game goes 1 … 16, 25 … 28, 18 … 22, 29 … 31, 23, 24, with the secret levels 7 and 17 on their routes as before. `next_level` applies a detour after the secret routes. The world map's files (`[MapFiles]`, "from level k on") follow the play order (`level_rank`): 25-28 show `MAP1.WLD` (from 15), 29-31 `MAP2.WLD` (from 21), 23-24 `MAP3.WLD`. A password still gives the level it encodes; the order after it is the same. GHOST and SHRINK are shortcuts that change this order (see Spells, Shortcuts); the password keeps them.

### Counts

40 exits lead somewhere, 24 of them doors. 2-1's upper exit and the doors into and out of side rooms or within a map arrive at entry points other than entry 1. VILLAGE map 7 (10-4) has no entry point, so Lomax arrives at its first revive point, where its start already was.

## Narrow maps

Two Flink maps are 480 px wide (5-1 = FOREST map 13, 24-2 = MOUNTAIN map 17), narrower than Lomax+'s widest view (512). The converter writes them at their own width, as every map; at widths 320 and 432 they scroll as any other map.

A map narrower than the view gets Lomax+'s narrowed view (`ws_narrowed` in `LOADER\dll\lomax.h`, `ws_bars` / `ws_inset_update` in `widescreen.c`): the camera centres it and black bars cover the rest, so at 512 the 480-px maps show with 16-px bars. Inside the bars the screen is the map's width: Lomax is kept 16 px from the bars (x 16 … map width − 16, as at 320), and the HUD's edge counters (the magic glass and its count, which follow the ammo's x, and the lives) and cheat mode's FPS and world.level texts stand inside them. Lomax+ makes one exception, the 26-tile (416-px) Evil Ed arena, which keeps its repeated floor.

The converter doesn't pad narrow maps: black padding tiles would show at width 320 too, and Lomax and the HUD would go into them at 512.

## What the converter writes

The output has three planes, in this order:

| Plane | Contents | Objects |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Flink's high-priority tiles, which the Mega Drive draws over sprites. Maps with a `Priority` actor have none here (see below) | A ScrollMap at 100/100, so it scrolls with the camera |
| 1 | The play map: the other tiles and the collision | Start over Flink's start (the revive point with parameter 1); Revive at the other revive points; Exit at the exits to the next map, or Finish over the `Warp`; Music with the map's Flink track (see Music); the platforms, enemies, chests, lianas and monkeys |
| 2 | The background map, made opaque: it shows Flink's backdrop colour where the map is transparent | A ScrollMap at 50% horizontally. Vertically it scrolls only as far as its height still covers the screen. |

- **Priority maps:** Flink's `Priority` actor (0x255E8) sets the priority bit of every tile of its map and of the sprites, so sprites are drawn over all the tiles. In its 8 maps (FOREST 8, 15, 16; MOUNTAIN 5, 8, 13, 17; META 6) every tile goes in the play plane, behind Lomax.
- **Draw order:** Lomax draws the first plane of a level in front. `Planes_UpdateDepth` gives the planes buckets 0x90, 0x40, 0x20 and 0x10, in order. Actors draw a few buckets above their own plane, so plane 1's actors appear over it and under plane 0.
- **Tiles:**
  - Flink cells are 4 bpp with four 16-colour palette lines, and one 16×16 tile can mix lines. Lomax builds each 16×16 tile with a single 256-colour palette.
  - The converter therefore stores the line in each pixel (`line*16 + colour`) and puts all 64 colours in palette 0. It makes one 8-bit cell per (cell, line) pair that is used.
- **Palettes:** Flink colours are `0000BBB0GGG0RRR0`. Lomax colours are 15-bit, with blue in bits 0–4 and red in bits 10–14.
  - Every plane is built with the play plane's palette, as on the Mega Drive.
  - Palettes 1–9 (Lomax's sprites) are copied from the template.
  - Some template sprites use palette 0, the level's own (the hint hat and hint guy, MEDIEVAL.WLD banks 38–45). Flink's colours fill entries 0–63, so the template colours those sprites use go into entries 64 and up, and their pixels are repointed there (`template_sprite_colours`).
- **Collision:** a Lomax collision byte is the top five bits of a Flink map word: `(word >> 8) & 0xF8`. Floors (`0xC0` plus a shape) and walls (`0x80` plus sides) use the same bits in both games.
- **Lomax-format details** that matter here:
  - every chunk size must be a multiple of 4;
  - TTAB is big-endian;
  - a type name over 15 characters is overwritten by the type's own fields.

  The full Lomax format is in `LOADER\docs\decompilation.md` and in the World_LoadWLD plate comment in Ghidra.

## Platforms

Flink's platforms become Lomax platform objects that draw Flink's own sprites:
- **Sprites:** Flink's `ANIM` frames, which are sets of hardware sprites, become Lomax `ANIM` banks. They are 8-bit, use palette 0, and get ids 100 and up.
- **Animations:** Flink's NMY animation records become Lomax records: the same pieces, offsets, flips, hitbox and animation speed. Both games count the speed the same way (0x100 = one frame per tick).
- **Types:** each platform kind gets a Lomax type whose first four letters select the Lomax behaviour: `Mega Flink n`, `Rota Flink n`, `Swin Flink n` or `Jump Flink n`. FLINK.DLL gives some kinds of Mega its own extra behaviour, named in the type: `Mega Float n` (platforms on the water, which ride the tide; see Water), `Mega Dip n` (Normalplatform), `Mega Fall n` (Tra2 / Trap platform), and `Mega Leaf n` / `Mega Leaves n` (Leaveplatform; see Leaves), `Mega Floating n` (Floating platform; see Floating platforms), `Mega Plant n` (Plant; see Plant).
- **Standing surface:** a Flink platform's hitbox, widened by 6 px on each side. Flink lands on a platform its body overlaps; Lomax lands only where his centre is, so a platform flush with the edge of a floor left a 1–2 px gap that he fell through when walking onto it.
- **Level with the floor:** Lomax steps onto a platform only if its top is level with or below his feet (`Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer`). A static or sinking platform whose top is up to 6 px above the floor beside it is lowered to the floor (the Trap platforms of MOUNTAIN map 7 sat 1 px above it).
- **Facing:** a platform Flink places facing the other way ("look left" / "look right") gets a mirrored copy of its type, with the pieces flipped and the hitbox's sides swapped. A Lomax placement can't set an actor's facing.

| Flink | Lomax | How |
|---|---|---|
| Normalplatform | Mega (MovingPlatform), static, dipping | When landed on it sinks 0.5 px/tick for 15 ticks and rises back, as Flink's (0x293F2). FLINK.DLL moves it (`Mega Dip`); without it the leaf is static. |
| Mega platform, Meg2 platform | Mega | The same motion: base + amplitude·sin(phase)/2, with the phase advancing by the speed each tick (1024 per turn). Both games use the same sine scale. The stalk (`APPE`) becomes the chain drawn every 32 px down to the bottom of the screen (`MovingPlatform_DrawWithChain` 0x429ED0). That loop stops one step short of the bottom, and Flink's stalk sprite hangs 16 px above its point, which left up to 16 px empty at the bottom, changing as the platform moved; each piece is drawn twice, 32 px apart. With "left/right" the platform moves horizontally. With "dont move" it sinks while stood on, at speed × 0x28F per tick, the same constant in both games, and also while a heavy thrown object (the big stone, an open chest) lies on it: Flink's think (0x2876A) calls `Platform_CarryThrownObjects` (0x1A1B2), which puts any heavy thrown object falling or at rest within 4 px of its top, inside its x, on that top (vx halved, vy 1/65536 down, an odd vy that keeps it from hitting anything), and the platform sinks. 10-1 is built on it: its pillar at x 1454 is too high to jump onto, and the stone thrown onto it brings it down. Lomax's sinking mode answers only to Lomax, so FLINK.DLL gives such a plain `Mega Flink` (sinking mode +0xA8, no rise +0xA4) `sink_update` (platforms.c: `MovingPlatform_Update`, then `grab_land_on` in grab.c, and the sink speed if any object lies there). Without it the stone would fall through the pillar: thrown objects land only on the map's tiles. |
| Sinkplatform | Mega, sinking | Sinks at 0.5 px/tick while stood on and rises again when left. On the water (the lilypads) it also rides the tide, as Flink's keeps to the water surface (0x2F1AE): it floats at the surface − 2 (think 0x2F1D4 never lets it above that and, left alone, raises it back 0.25 px a frame), so the converter places it there rather than at its placement (7 px lower in 2-1). |
| Tra2 / Trap platform | Mega, falling | As Flink's (think 0x2768E): param 0 ticks after Lomax lands on it, it starts to fall, its speed growing 1/8 px/tick each tick up to 5 px/tick, and it never stops, ridden or not, until it is gone below the map. With param −1 (3 in level 41) it starts when Lomax comes within 20 px of its sides and 70 px above to 10 px below its top. FLINK.DLL moves it (`Mega Fall`, `fall_update`); the converter puts the delay in the Y speed. |
| Seesaw | Mega, static, tilting | The seesaw: see Seesaws. FLINK.DLL tilts it and throws Lomax (`Mega Seesaw`, `seesaw.c`). |
| Leaveplatform ("lonely leave") | Mega, falling leaf | As Flink's: stood on, it comes off and falls, swaying from side to side and carrying Lomax, until it is under the map; it comes back at its placement. See Leaves. |
| Leaveplatform (leaf spawner) | Mega, unseen | As Flink's: drops such a falling leaf from 80 px above it every 64 frames. See Leaves. |
| Swingplatform / Swing platform with arms | Rota (wheel) | The same wheel: radius (length − 1) × 16 px, arms evenly spaced. The speed is halved, because Flink counts 2048 steps per turn and Lomax 1024. "dont move" makes it turn only while ridden, ramping up at the same rate (1/16 step per tick). FLINK.DLL's `dll\wheel.c` runs Flink's think instead: its exact speed, its `pre-run`, META's `stretch` (the clouds coming in toward the pivot and going back out) and Flink's chains (see Swings, Wheels). Lomax also scatters leaves (`BLAD`) along spokes and ropes (`Draw_RopeSegments` 0x425D40); Flink's chains have none, so they are blank. |
| Swingplatform / Swing platform with 0 arms, Trunk, harmless swinging balls | Swin (pendulum) | Flink's pendulum, with its bounce (`winkel`) and, for the Trunk (the logs), its two parallel chains: FLINK.DLL `swing.c` runs and draws it as Flink's code. See Swings. |
| Bouncy platform | Mega, `Mega Bouncy` | TREE's leaf on a stalk: the Jumppad's throw at frame 7 of its `JUMP` animation, with its `x-speed` sideways (see Jumppads, Bouncy platforms). |
| Exploding platform | Mega, `Mega Explode` | As Flink's: at rest until stood on, then it goes sideways for good, showing the seconds left, and breaks up when they run out (see Exploding platforms). |
| Wolky platform (cloud) | Mega, `Mega Wolky` | As Flink's: it rises off the top of the map, swaying, and its Generator brings it back (see Wolky platforms). |
| Floating platform | Mega, unseen | As Flink's: a current of leaves blowing left across the screen, bobbing up and down and carrying Lomax. See Floating platforms. |
| Wall platform | Mega, `Mega Wall` | A brick sliding out of the wall and back on timers, standable only while out (see Wall platforms). |
| Rock | Mega, `Mega Rock`, pushed | The block of stones Lomax pushes as he pushes his barrel; it falls off ledges (see Rocks). |
| Steer platform | Mega, ridden down | As Flink's (0x28CBC; one, in 27-1): see Steer platform. FLINK.DLL `steer.c` (`Mega Steer`). |
| Wagon | Mega, `Mega Wagon`, ridden | The Wagon on the rails (18-2, 20-1): see Wagons. FLINK.DLL `wagon.c`. |
| Hand | Mega, closes on a timer | As Flink's (0x28696; 3-3, 6-2): see Hands. FLINK.DLL `hand.c` (`Mega Hand`). |
| Plant (the Plant) | Mega, grows | Stood on, Ability and 4 spirits make it grow up to its height; left alone it sinks back. See Plant. FLINK.DLL `plant.c` (`Mega Plant`). |
| Throw platform | Mega, carried | As Flink's: it lies at its placement; Lomax picks it up (heavy) and throws it. In the water it floats at the surface + 5, riding the tide, and drifts the way it was thrown at |param| × 0x28F per tick until a wall stops it for good; it dips when landed on, and it can be stood on, on land too. A `Mega Throw` (FLINK.DLL `throwplat.c`, see Grabbing and throwing). |

Two Flink actors that aren't platforms are converted too, because Lomax would fall through the map without them:

| Flink | Lomax | How |
|---|---|---|
| Deadly ground (spikes, 0x28498) | Floor, and `Spik Deadly` | A strip x ± param that hurts Flink. Its spikes are tiles over a gap in the floor, so Lomax fell through into the water. The strip gets one-way floor at the level of the floor beside it, except in columns that already have collision there (`floor_along`): at the gap's ends Flink's map has solid blocks with only bit 13 or bit 12 set, walls against moving right or left, at which Flink's walking enemies turn back. A one-way cell put in the row above those columns and above the slopes next to them made a ledge 8–15 px over the slope in 3-1, and the Ingredient Holder fell through the ground there into the water. The strip also gets a `Spik Deadly` object: Lomax's `Spik` (object 51) is a decoration, which FLINK.DLL turns into a hazard calling `Player_TakeHit` while Lomax is in the strip (it knocks the helmet off, or kills him without it). |
| Bridge (0x27C7E) | Mega, `Mega Bridge` | Flink's 12 planks, sagging up to 9 px, each falling half a second after Lomax steps on it (see Bridges). |

The 45 deadly swinging balls (checkbox 8) are hazards, not platforms: see "Swinging balls".

**Chain links.** The pendulums' links are drawn by `swing.c` as Flink's (see Swings). Lomax's swing and wheel draw their links with `Draw_RopeSegments` (0x425D40), one every `step` px from the pivot; `Swin_Swing_Draw` (5 calls) and `Rota_RotatingPlatforms_DrawSpokes` (0x41F672) push a step of 8. Flink's chain draw (0x2B752, for both kinds) puts a link every 16 px (0x40 of its ¼ px units), from 16 px out to the platform. In Flink's maps FLINK.DLL patches the six pushes to 16 (`chains_set`, from `world_load`; back to 8 for Lomax's levels): the same number of links as Flink's, from the pivot instead of from 16 px out. With 8 the links overlapped.

## Leaves

Flink's `Leaveplatform` (init 0x2B9F0) is the big leaf on the Trees of FOREST: 18 "lonely leave" leaves (3-1, 5-1, 8-1) and one leaf spawner (3-1, over the water pit at x 938). FLINK.DLL `leaves.c` runs Flink's code, at a Flink frame per Lomax tick:
- **At rest** (think 0x2BB24) it is a platform (`STAY`). The tick Lomax stands on it, it starts to fall (`FALL`). Its fall speed is 0.5 or 0.625 px a tick, picked at random when it spawns; its drift is 0.25 px a tick to the left, to the right when flipped ("look left", checkbox 1).
- **Falling** (think 0x2BB5C), with Flink's sine table (0x1491E, 1024 entries of amplitude 0x4000, the same as round(0x4000 · sin)):
  - a sway phase steps 1/128 of a turn a tick: x speed = sin(sway) × 4 / 65536 + the drift, up to 1.25 px a tick. An unflipped leaf starts at half a turn (it swings left first), a flipped one at 0;
  - the frame is |((sway + (flipped ? 0 : half a turn)) mod a turn) − half a turn| / 0x15F, 0–2 of `FALL`: 2 (as at rest) at the ends of a sway, 0 in its middle, so the leaf tilts as it swings;
  - a bob phase steps 1/64 of a turn a tick: y speed = sin(bob) / 65536 + the fall speed (0.25 to 0.875 px a tick down);
  - it moves through walls and floors and carries Lomax (`Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` 0x413510, Flink's 0x1A28A), and at the map's height + 20 px it is despawned (`Actor_Despawn`, which lets its placement spawn it again, as Flink's 0x32D8E).
- **The spawner** (think 0x2BA74, drawn as nothing) drops the n-th leaf every 64 ticks at its x + {−90, 0, −60, 30, −30, 60, 0, 90}[n mod 8] and 80 px above it: falling at once at 0.5 px a tick, no drift, both phases random ((random & 0x3F) × 16 and twice that), flipped when bit 6 of the bob phase is set. Flink gives the spawner and its leaves its widest activation window; here the spawner is never culled and drops leaves while within 256 px of the view, and a leaf is despawned under the map or farther than 256 px from the view.

Converter: a lonely leaf is a static `Mega Leaf n` with records `MAIN` (`STAY`) and `FALL`, mirrored when flipped, with param 2 = 1 (the Mega's x speed, unused with no amplitude), never put on the tide. The spawner is a `Mega Leaves n` at its placement whose `MAIN` is `FALL`; its leaves are `Actor_Alloc` children of its type, mirrored with the actor's facing flag, so their standing surface stays unmirrored (`FALL`'s is 3 px off centre).

## Floating platforms

Flink's `Floating platform` (init 0x2B818; params area, timer, speed, pre-run) is the current of leaves that blows across TREE's 25-2 (one at (525, 327), spawned by the Generator at x 327) and 26-1 (x 318, 1329 and 1809). The placement is a spawner drawn as nothing; FLINK.DLL `floating.c` runs Flink's code, at a Flink frame per Lomax tick:
- **The spawner** (think 0x2B876) works while its x − the camera's x is within −area … 400; elsewhere it clears its pre-run. While it works, the pre-run counts down, and every `timer` frames (a quarter of that while the pre-run lasts; the first after 3) it sends a leaf (`FLY`) from x + area + 420 (always off Flink's screen to the right) and its y + (random & 15), at (speed + (random & 3)) / 100 px a frame to the left.
- **A leaf** (think 0x2B932) bobs: a phase (random at the start) steps 1/128 of a turn a frame and its y speed is sin(phase), up to 1 px a frame (about 20 px up and down), with `FLY`'s frame 0 while it rises faster than 0.25 px a frame, 2 while it sinks faster, 1 in between. On the screen it moves carrying Lomax (`Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` 0x413510, Flink's 0x1A28A), through walls and floors; off it, it moves 4 times as fast across while its spawner's pre-run lasts, so the pre-run fills the current with leaves at their usual spacing before it is seen. Off the screen and left of the camera it is gone.
- **Here:** Flink spawns the spawner when its window 0 reaches it (where the 400 holds); Lomax spawns placements farther ahead, which would clear the pre-run, so a spawner spawned by proximity waits until Flink's window reaches it (`flink_window`, enemy.c); a Generator's starts at once. The camera is Flink's (`flink_cam_x`: the player's x − 0xA4 within the map, or the Scroll Point's), so the leaves start where Flink's do, 256 px or more right of Lomax: off the screen at Lomax+'s widest view, 512. A leaf's on-screen test is Lomax's view (`enemy_on_screen`), and it goes once it is off that view on the left. Spawner and leaves are never culled; the leaves are `Actor_Alloc` children of its type on Lomax's layer for Flink's draw list 2.

Converter: a `Mega Floating n` at the placement, drawn as nothing, whose `MAIN` is `FLY` (its leaves'), params 5–8 Flink's area, timer, speed and pre-run.

## Plant

Flink's `Plant` (init 0x2E3B0, think 0x2E412, draw 0x2E4AC; 6 in FOREST: 3-3, 3-4, 3-5, 8-1 and two in 7-1) is a leaf (`LEAF`) on a stalk (`ROOT`). In Flink, standing on it and casting QUICK GROW (spell 4, 4 magic; with 4 or less only the gauge flashes) makes it grow at 1 px a frame up to its param ("height", 40 to 290 px), carrying Flink; left alone, it sinks back home at 1 px a frame, so a second cast costs again. Its stalk is drawn from the ground under its placement (`Map_GetGroundHeight` 0x1AEEA within 100 px, + 2) up to the leaf: `ROOT`'s two frames alternating every 16 px (the second is the first turned over), the alternation flipping with the leaf's own 2-frame animation.

Here QUICK GROW is chosen on the spell wheel (see Spells), and Lomax standing on the plant casts it by pressing **Ability** (the grab/throw button; Lomax has no spell button and no casting gesture, so it grows at once). It costs **4 spirits** (Flink's 4 magic); with fewer the cost on the HUD flickers red. With another spell chosen the press is a pick-up. The press is taken before a pick-up (`grab_before` in grab.c calls `plant_grow`), needs a new press (holding Ability while landing on it does nothing), only when Lomax carries nothing (a press then throws), and only while the plant is at rest below its top: Flink's would take a cast at the top and grow on past it. No sparkles (Flink's 0x2ECA6) and no sound.

Converter: `plat_plant`, a static `Mega Plant n` with records `MAIN` (`LEAF`) and `ROOT`; params: the stalk's foot below the placement in the X speed (`plant_foot`, measured after the placement's final y) and the height in the Y speed, both with an amplitude of 0. FLINK.DLL: `dll\plant.c` (`plant_update` moves it with `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer`, `plant_draw` draws the leaf, then each stalk piece through `Actor_Draw`, `plant_grow`).

## Scrolling screens

Some of Flink's maps move the camera on their own at a fixed pace, or keep it from following Flink on one axis. Flink does it with the `Scroll Point` (MAIN.WLD's system type 107, 0x2E628): 13 in 12 maps. It runs once, sets the camera's state and removes itself (0x32D9E); the state stays until another Scroll Point changes it or the map ends.

| Map | Scroll Point | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| 3-2 | at the start: x 0.5 px, y kept | the screen scrolls right over the Snake's lake |
| 9-2 | Generator: x 0.5 px | scrolls right from where its Generator fires |
| 12-1 | at the start: x 0.5 px, height 128 | scrolls right |
| 16-2 | at the start: x 1.0 px, y kept, height 200 | the Village Boss's fight: the map wraps around and the screen scrolls on (see Enemies, The Village Boss); once it is gone, a short pan to the right edge |
| 21-1 | at the start: x 0.5 px, height 96; Generator at x 2401: x kept | scrolls right over the falling platforms, then stops at the end |
| 27-1 | Generator: y 0.45 px | scrolls down the tree's shaft, ridden on the Steer platform |
| 28-1 | Generator: x 1.0 px | scrolls right from where its Generator fires |
| 8-1, 15-2 | at the start: y kept | |
| 8-2 | at the start: y kept, height 208 | |
| 14-1 | Generator: x kept | |
| 26-1 | at the start: height 96 | |

**Flink** (its params, checkboxes and `Camera_Update` 0x19E12):
- param 2 in 0x40..0xDC: Flink's height on the screen (0x872E, 0xA0 by default); the camera jumps there (0x19DC4);
- checkbox bit 0: the camera's x stays (0x8723); bit 1: its y stays (0x8722, then no lower than the map's height − 0xE8);
- params 0 and 1 × 0x28F: the camera's x and y speeds (0x8730 / 0x8734, in hundredths of a px a frame; 0 = follow Flink). The camera's own speed goes toward them by 0x200 a frame, and is 0 again at the map's edges (it stops at the map's width − 320; the wrap flag 0x8726 is never set). A kept axis wins over a scrolling one.
- While the x scrolls, Flink stays 26–294 px inside the 320-px screen (0x1CCCC). While the y scrolls down and Flink is left above the screen, he dies (0x1BB40). Falling 273 px below the camera's y is the ordinary pit (0x1BBA6), scrolling or not.

**Lomax's own** is the airship's scrolling screen, `ForceScrolling` (object 8, `ForceScroll_Init` 0x41C270, `ForceScroll_Update` 0x41C2E0). It uses the same unit (param × 0x28F) and sets player state 0x20 (`Player_Update` leaves the camera's x alone), 0x8000000 (nor keeps Lomax inside the screen) and 0x20000000 (the map wraps around, for the airship). Each tick it moves the camera's x, keeps Lomax 16–304 px inside the screen (Lomax+ widens the 304 with the view: 416 at width 432), carries him along at the camera's speed when he is pinned at an edge, and kills him squeezed between the left edge and a wall (`g_PlayerWallHit` 2). It has no y, no end (once the speed is reached it moves 1 px a tick, whatever the speed) and no locks.

**Here.** The converter writes each Scroll Point as a `Forc Flink` (object 8), params x speed, y speed, height and the checkboxes. One set at the map's start (Flink's spawn flag bit 6) gets Lomax's placement flag 0x4000, spawned at every start and restart; the others are linked to their Generators (see Hidden objects). FLINK.DLL `scroll.c`:
- object 8's spawn (`force_spawn`) sets Flink's state from the params and the actor removes itself; Lomax's `ForceScroll_Init` doesn't run in Flink's maps (it would wrap the map), and Lomax's levels keep it;
- before `Player_Update` (`player_tick`), state 0x20 / 0x40 keep each axis that scrolls or stays from Lomax's camera, and 0x8000000 is set while the x scrolls;
- after it, the x moves at Flink's speed and ramp up to the plane's `nMaxScrollX`, then `ForceScroll_Update` runs with a stand-in actor whose speed is reached and the camera 1 px short, so Lomax's own code adds that pixel, keeps Lomax on the screen and crushes him against walls; the y moves at its speed up to `nMaxScrollY`, and Lomax left above the screen while it scrolls down dies (whatever he wears);
- Flink's height becomes `g_CameraPlayerOffsetY` + 8 (Lomax's view is 240 px high, Flink's 224), which Lomax's own camera follows; the camera jumps there.
- a map that scrolls from the start (3-2, 12-1, 16-2, 21-1) starts with Lomax standing on the floor under the Start trapdoor, as a secret level, with no drop and no "Let's go": during the drop player state 0x40 held the camera's y at the trapdoor while the x scrolled on, so the screen was off until he landed. `force_spawn` calls `trapdoor_skip_drop` (exits.c), which runs `secret_start` on the trapdoor of this first start and puts the camera on him.
- **Wrap** (`scroll_wrap`, from the Village Boss: Flink's 0x8726). The camera's x no longer stops at the map's edges, and player state 0x20000000 (Lomax's airship's wrap) is set: Lomax's tile layers always repeat across (`TileLayer_Draw` takes cells modulo the width), and with the state so do the ground and the walls (`Plane_FindGroundY`, `Actor_CheckWallCollision`). Lomax's 16.16 positions would overflow at 32768 px, so past x 16384 the camera, Lomax and every actor of the plane go back 8192 px, a whole number of the map's and the half-speed background's widths (512 px both): nothing shows, and Lomax+'s FPS Override doesn't interpolate a jump that big. `scroll_stop_x` (the Village Boss defeated) holds the camera's x.
- The state goes at every map load and every start and restart (a lost life doesn't load the map again; the Start trapdoor's spawn clears it, and it comes before the Scroll Points in the plane's list).

## Steer platform

Flink's `Steer platform` (init 0x28CBC, `CONT`; one, at the top of 27-1's shaft) is the ride down the tree under the screen scrolling down. FLINK.DLL `steer.c`, at a Flink frame per Lomax tick:
- at rest (0x28CE6) until Lomax stands on it; then it sinks 0.5 px a tick for good (the screen scrolls 0.45), through floors, and is despawned under the map;
- it steers by where he stands (0x28D06): its x speed goes toward (dx + 12) / 16 px a tick ((dx − 12) / 16 when dx < 0; dx = his x − its x) by 0x800 a tick, and a wall at its front side stops it (0x1AD4C: the cells at its x ± 29 px, `CONT`'s hitbox side without the converter's 6 px, read from Lomax's collision cells as `Actor_CheckWallCollision` does). Off it, the steering speed falls back to 0 by 0x100 a tick;
- it tilts: frame 0 with him 8 px or more right of its x, 2 with him 8 px or more left, else 1, and after a tilt the surface under him is dx >> 3 px lower at frame 0 and −(dx >> 3) at frame 2 (an arithmetic shift, as 0x28D5C);
- Lomax stands on it while his x is within −32 … 35 px of its x: `CONT`'s hitbox (−26 … 29, Flink's test of the player's x, 0x1A304) widened by the converter's 6 px each side, as every converted platform's. It carries him firmly (his feet are put on its surface each tick, as Flink's).
- **Walking on it** is Flink's walk (`steer_before` / `steer_after`, around Player_Update): Flink walks on a platform as on flat ground (0x1C4A6), at most 1.0 px a frame for the first 60 frames a direction is held (0x828F, set again while no direction is held and at each turn), then speeding up by 0x800 a frame to 2.0 (0x1BDB6, rows 0x1D004 and 0x1CFC4). While Lomax stands on it his walking limit (`g_PlayerSpeedCapOverride`, as the Rock's push) is 1.0 for those 60 ticks, then rises by 0x800 a tick to 2.0; his acceleration and friction stay Lomax's. Lomax's own walk (2.4 px a tick, reached at 0.125 a tick) would take him off its 61 px in about 15 ticks, before the leaf has picked up 0.5 px a tick of steering: the slow first second is what lets small steps steer it.
- **The Raketen wurms** of the map stay in their mud until Lomax has stood on it since the last start (`steer_waiting`, wurm.c). Flink's come out by Lomax's x alone (0x25B94), and the ones that do before the ride are below Flink's screen: Flink's camera is the player's y − 160 (0x872E), 224 px high, and Lomax's his y − 112, 240 px high, so here the shaft's walls under the ledge show (the Raketen wurm at (140, 298) comes out with Lomax at x 20–80 at the start and at 200–260 on the jump to the leaf).

Converter: a static `Mega Steer n` with `CONT`'s three frames as `MAIN`.

## Wagons

Flink's `Wagon` (init 0x23A4C, think 0x23AA0, draws 0x23DE4 / 0x23E0A; MOUNTAIN only) is the Wagon on the rails: 18-2 has two (x 175 and 1024), 20-1 two (x 898, "deadly", and 1203, spawned by the Generator at 1409). Params: `start-speed` and `max-speed` (× 0x28F a frame: 100 = 1.0 px), `distance` (its end is that far right of where it spawned) and the checkbox `deadly`. FLINK.DLL `wagon.c` runs Flink's think, a Flink frame a Lomax tick:
- **The ground:** it stands on the rails (0x1AE96: the ground from y − 16 down 3 cells, within 3 px at rest and up to 10 at speed, the table 0x1AECA by its speed's whole px). Level (`EINS`), its speed goes toward 0 by 0x40 a frame; on a slope (`ZWEI` falling to the right, `DREI` to the left) it speeds up downhill by 0x800 a frame up to `max-speed` and slows by 0x200 uphill. Off the rails it falls, + 0x800 a frame (1/8 of an actor's usual gravity), and keeps its speed. It faces the way it goes; its wheels turn at |vx| / 1000 (at most a frame every 4 ticks).
- **Getting in:** with Lomax's feet 32 px or more above its y and within 24 px across it becomes a platform (and stays one while he is lower, in it or beside it): its hitbox top (13 px above its bottom level, 14 tilted, 1 px lower on the bounce frame). Landing in it at rest, the Wagon moves to his x and goes at `start-speed`.
- **Riding:** his x is the Wagon's, also in the air, so a jump in it comes back down into it; Left and Right are the Wagon's (taken out of his input): Right speeds it up by 0x400 a frame to `max-speed`, Left slows it by 0x400 while it goes faster than 2.0. He faces the way it goes. The Wagon is drawn twice: its back wall (hide selector 1) behind Lomax and its front and wheels (selector 2) in front of him, so he stands in it; empty, it is drawn whole in front of him.
- **Getting out:** a Wagon off the ground lets go of a Lomax in the air (his x is free; he has the speed he had: none). Landing elsewhere, or dying, he is out with the Wagon's speed.
- **The end:** at its end x Lomax in it is thrown out, 2.0 up with the Wagon's speed; a `deadly` one he stands on hurts him (`Player_TakeHit`). Then a Wagon on the screen (Flink's actor +6 bit 4, set by `Actors_DrawAll` 0x334FE while some of it was drawn) breaks up, Flink's 0x1B1F8 (18-2's second Wagon breaks at the tree trunk, x 1904): each piece of its current frame flies off from where it was drawn at (rand & 31 − 15) / 8 px across and 1.0 + (rand & 31) / 8 px up, falling 0x4000 a frame, with sound 0x2A (here Lomax's EXPLOS2 low, 0x58, as the Village Boss's 0x2A); `actor_break_up` (wagon.c, shared with the exploding platforms) fills `Actor_ShatterIntoPieces`' piece layout (at most 17 pieces; the Wagon's frames have 3: wheels, rim, planks) and lets `Shatter_DrawPieces` draw them and despawn it once none is on screen. Broken, it isn't spawned again until a lost life (Flink leaves its pending bit 0x6400 clear; here `A_DEF` is cleared, so the despawn leaves its placement spawned). A Wagon off the screen is only despawned (0x32D8E) and comes back at its placement.
- **Sound:** Flink's rolling sound (0x38, PCM 41, when it starts rolling on the ground) plays while it is on the screen (+6 bit 4), stopped when it leaves the ground (Pcm 0x3FE4A, d6 0x29): `sfx_loop` (see Sound effects).

**Lomax's jump in it.** Lomax's `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` moves a player it still rides with before its landing test, and lands him during his jump's wind-up (`g_JumpTakeoffTimer`, his vy counted as 0), so a jump from the Wagon never left it. Flink's 0x1A29A doesn't land a rising player, and Flink's Player_Update clears his platform in the air: here the Wagon carries Lomax through the wind-up (he stands on it while he crouches) and, once he rises, doesn't carry him and clears `g_pRidingPlatform`.

**Where they go.** By Flink's think, three of the four leave their rails where the next ground is higher and fall out of the map: 18-2's first leaves at (402, 208) at 2.5 px a frame and the next rails start at x 544, y 176; 20-1's Wagons leave at about (1760, 272), the ground ahead at y 160. 18-2's second leaves at (1746, 176) and reaches its end, x 1904, in the air at y 227, throwing Lomax out over the pit (the next rails at x 2080; an island at x 1888-1984, y 112, and a liana at x 1846 overhead). The falls match Flink's.

Converter: `plat_wagon`, a static `Mega Wagon n` with `EINS` as `MAIN`, `ZWEI` and `DREI`, their hide selectors kept, Flink's hitbox widened by 1 px a side (Flink's test includes the box's sides, Lomax's doesn't), not lowered to the floor nor put on the tide; 9 params, Lomax's 5 (0) and Flink's four.

## Exploding platforms

Flink's `Exploding platform` (init 0x2397A, think 0x239C0, digit hook 0x23A12; MOUNTAIN only: 13 in 19-2, 2 in 23-1) is a rock ledge over a pit. Params: `speed` (× 0x28F a frame, signed: 50 = 0.5 px to the right) and `time` (seconds). FLINK.DLL `explode.c` runs Flink's think, a Flink frame a Lomax tick:
- **At rest** it stands where it is placed (`EINS`), a platform through `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` (Flink's 0x1A28A, its surface the hitbox top).
- **Stood on** the first time, its countdown starts at `time` × 60 − 1 frames and it goes at `speed`, for good: nothing stops it (walls neither), whether Lomax stays on it or not. While it counts, `ZAHL`'s frame countdown / 60 (1 … 5, the seconds left) is drawn on its face at `EINS`'s attach point (0, 10), in front of it (Flink's hook for the attach point, the frame's first piece; here a second `Actor_Draw` of the actor with `ZAHL`, so that Lomax+'s FPS Override moves it with the platform).
- **At 0** it takes `BOOM` and breaks up as the Wagon (Flink's 0x1B1F8, `actor_break_up`: its 10 pieces fly off and fall, sound 0x58 for Flink's 0x2A); Lomax on it falls. Broken, it isn't spawned again until a lost life.
- A rising Lomax isn't carried (as the Wagon: Lomax's carry would hold his feet on it through a jump).
- Draw list 3 ((a5) = 0x7D70): Lomax's layer 0.

19-2's map also has four one-way floor cells with no tile at x 672-735 (a floor at y 144 between the platforms at 584 and 777) that Flink's `Map_GetGroundHeight` reads; nothing shows a floor there, so the converter takes them out (`NO_COLLISION`).

Converter: `plat_exploding`, a static `Mega Explode n` with `EINS` as `MAIN`, `BOOM` and `ZAHL`; 7 params, Lomax's 5 (0), `speed` and `time`.

## Wolky platforms

Flink's `Wolky platform` (init 0x230E4, think 0x23120; META) is a cloud that rises off the top of the map. Param: `speed` (× 0x28F a frame upward: 100 = 1 px). FLINK.DLL `wolky.c` runs Flink's think, a Flink frame a Lomax tick:
- **Rising**: `WOLK`, its sway phase + 8 a frame and x speed sin(phase) (table 0x1491E, 1024 a turn, 0.25 px at most: 5 px each way every 128 frames), moved by its speeds and carrying Lomax (`Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer`, Flink's 0x1A28A).
- **Landed on**: the frame Lomax lands on it, it sinks at its speed for 20 frames, then rises again with him.
- **Gone** at y −30 or above, or outside Flink's mid window (activation window 8), with `Actor_Despawn` (Flink's 0x32D8E: pending again), so its Generator spawns it again at its placement while Lomax is in the Generator's box. 30-2's start has three, from the bottom of the map at 1, 0.75 and 0.5 px a frame, each back as soon as it is gone: a rising staircase of clouds that never ends while Lomax waits at the Generator.
- Draw list 3 ((a5) = 0x7D70): Lomax's layer 0.

The Blitz dude's cloud, let go, runs the same think (`wolky_step`, shared with blitz.c).

Converter: `plat_wolky`, a static `Mega Wolky n` with `WOLK` as `MAIN`; 6 params, Lomax's 5 (0) and `speed`.

## Hands

Flink's `Hand` (init 0x28696; 3 in 3-3, 2 in 6-2) is a wooden hand on an arm: a Mega platform (it goes up and down by its speed and amplitude, or left and right with checkbox 2) that closes into a fist on a timer. Every "period" frames (param 2: 200 in 3-3, 25 in 6-2) it closes for 90 frames: `MEGA`'s three frames, open to fist, a frame every 4, held on the fist, then open again at once. Closing, it plays Flink's sound 0x36 (ROCKHITX here) when on screen, and if Lomax stood on it the tick before it hurts him (Player_TakeHit, knocked away from it). While closed it is no platform, but Lomax bounces off the fist when he falls onto it, as off an enemy (in Flink it only clanks); touching it from the side hurts only a "deadly" one (checkbox 4, 6-2's). Lomax's thrown helmet opens a closed fist, and it then stays open 420 frames, as Flink's after a spell or slide hits it.

Converter: `plat_hand`, a `Mega Hand n` with `MEGA` as `MAIN` and `APPE` (the arm) as the Mega's chain, drawn down to the screen's bottom; the period goes into the speed of the axis it doesn't move on (amplitude 0 there), bit 15 = "deadly". FLINK.DLL `hand.c`: `hand_update` runs `MovingPlatform_Update`; while closed, with player state 0x20000 set around it, so that `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` moves it without carrying or landing Lomax, and with the fist flagged hurts + hittable, so that Player_Update's contact loop bounces Lomax off it (`Player_BounceOffEnemy`) and the helmet marks it hit (+0x58 bit 0x20000); `hand_harmless` (contact.c's `contact_hit`) keeps a side touch harmless unless it is deadly.

## Wall platforms

Flink's `Wall platform` (init 0x2897E, think 0x289BC; VILLAGE: 3 in 11-1, a staircase, and 3 in 13-1, over the pit after the second spikes) is a brick that slides out of the wall and back on timers.

- Params: `in` frames, `out` frames, `pre-run` frames (13-1's: 50, 75 and 80, 40, 0, so they go in turn). Its record `WALL` has 5 frames, in the wall (0) to out (4).
- It starts going in, at frame 0, the countdown at `in`; then the step runs `pre-run` times.
- **Each frame:** at frame 3 or 4 it is a platform (0x1A28A: its hitbox's top, landed on from above); then the step: when the countdown runs out the phase flips (`in` frames going in, `out` coming out), and every 4th frame (the countdown & 3 = 0) the frame goes one toward 0 or 4.

Each becomes a `Mega Wall n`, a static Lomax moving platform (object 64; converter `plat_wall`) with `WALL`'s frames 0–4 as `MAIN` and 8 params: Lomax's five (0) and Flink's three. FLINK.DLL's `dll\wall.c` (`wall_spawn` from `mega_spawn`) holds the frame and runs `MovingPlatform_Update`, at frames 0–2 with player state 0x20000 set around it (as the Hand's closed fist), so Lomax isn't carried or landed: standing on a brick that slides in, he falls.

## Rocks

Flink's `Rock` (init 0x2917C, think 0x291EC) is a block of one, two or three stones (`EINS`, `ZWEI` with checkbox 2, `DREI` with checkbox 4) that Flink pushes. 7 are converted: 21-1's two breakable ones (below), 11-1's at x 1583 (two stones, pushed off its ledge and down the slope to make a step up), 11-2's at 275 (pushed into the spike pit to stand on) and at 1114 (one stone, dropped by the Generator at (1222, 31) onto the slope), 19-2's (dropped by a Generator) and 24-1's.

- **Flink's think:** it takes Flink's facing and tests for a wall at that side (0x1AD4C: the column at its x + its hitbox's right, from y − 1 up |top| / 16 + 2 cells): a wall sets vx 1, "it doesn't move". It stands on the ground under its x (0x1AE96: within 3 cells, and within 3 px, the table at 0x1AEDA for vx 0), or falls (0x1AE3E: + 0.25 a frame up to 4.0, landing on ground met between the old and the new y, sound 0x35). Flink stands on it (0x1A28A), and then, falling or not, `Actor_CheckPushedByPlayer` (0x1AA56) may make it the pushed object (`g_PushedActor` 0x8300): not while dying; on the ground with the pad held away from it, no; the point 16 px in front of Flink (one more px plus his speed's whole px when both move, the table at 0x1AA96) inside its hitbox across, his feet not above its top + |its vy| + 1 and his feet − 32 not below its bottom. Met for the first time (last frame's 0x8302) with vx 0, Flink's speed is divided by 16.
- **Flink's Player_Update** (0x1C31C, after his x moved): the offset is 16 px from the hitbox's near side. On the ground the rock is moved to Flink's x + the offset, and let go when he stops; with vx 1 (a wall) he is put at its side instead; carrying something, he is put at its side and lets go. In the air: his feet − 30 at or below its bottom (under it) push him down 2 × |vy| + 2 px with vy 2.0 down; rising, he is put at its side and lets go; falling, he lets go and moves 2 px away from it, his speed toward it taken off. Then `PUSH` (0x1CB16) while it is pushed.
- **Push speed:** `PUSH`'s animation code (0x1CB20) restarts Flink's 60-frame turn timer (0x828F) every frame, and while it runs his movement takes PlayerSpeedTable's timer rows (0x1D004 on flat ground: −1.0 / 1.0, accel 0x2400): he pushes at 1.0 px a frame, half his 2.0 walk. So a rock pushed over a ledge's end keeps being pushed while it starts to fall, until its top passes below his feet: it tips off the edge rather than dropping straight down.
- **Thrown objects** that hit it are turned back (mirrored, vx negated, moved 2 × the new vx), or, at its top (within 8 px) and falling, bounced up at 2.0 with 1.5 × their vx (at least 2.0); sound 0x29 (0x29252: the thrown objects' vy is up-positive in Flink).
- **Breakable** (checkbox 8, `razorback-check`: 21-1's two, at x 833 on the ledge, 4 hits, and at 1924 on the ground, 20 hits). Param 0 is the hits it takes: every hit from anything but a running Razorback counts it down, and below 0 it breaks (0x293BC: `TOD1` / `TOD2` / `TOD3`, its stones flying apart, Actor_ForgetForever with the pending bit set back, so it is there again after a lost life). A Razorback that runs into it has already died in its own think when the rock looks at the hit, so every Razorback counts (the 5th breaks the ledge's rock, the 21st the other); thrown objects count too (once a bounce here; Flink counts each frame of the hit, which the bounce ends). The player as the hitter breaks it only while casting a spell (0x828E bit 7), which Lomax can't. A dead Razorback is turned back by it as a thrown object is. The converter gives it params Lomax's 5, then breakable and the hits, and its `TODn` frame as `BOOM`; `rock_hit` / `rock_break` in rock.c, the break-up is wagon.c's `actor_break_up`.
- **Not ported:** checkbox 16 'touch down' (breaks when it lands): none in the converted maps; the converter leaves such a rock out.

Lomax's own push (`Actor_CheckPlayerPush` 0x4131C0 and Player_Update's `g_pPushBlock` code at 0x411174, as his barrel uses them) isn't used: it slows pushing to 1.0 px a tick (`g_PlayerMoveTable` row 8), and in the air it pulls Lomax 2 px a tick toward the block's side, so he would stick to a falling rock and be drawn to it when jumping near one. Only his `PUSH` pose is Lomax's; the sound is Flink's rolling 0x38 (see Sound effects). The push speed is Flink's 1.0: `rock_check` sets Lomax's `g_PlayerSpeedCapOverride` (0x49C4B4, which Player_Update takes as the walking limit instead of `g_PlayerMoveTable`'s; the barrel sets it) for that tick while he pushes on the ground, and `rock_after` clears it.

One change from Flink: a touch in the air while Lomax moves away from the rock doesn't count. In Flink a jump away from a rock he stands against meets it again in the air (his x has hardly moved yet), which puts him back at its side, and since that lets go, every frame is a first touch that divides his speed by 16; Lomax, slower to turn round in the air, stayed stuck to it.

The converter's `plat_rock` makes each a `Mega Rock n`, a static Lomax moving platform (object 64) whose hitbox is Flink's, not widened (Lomax pushes from its side). FLINK.DLL `dll\rock.c` (`rock_spawn` from `mega_spawn`) replaces its update with `rock_update`, Flink's think: the wall test at the side Lomax faces (the map's edge counts as a wall, which Flink's test ignores), the ground or the fall (Lomax's landing sound 0x58 for 0x35, as the drop balls'), `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` (which carries Lomax standing on it) and `rock_check`, which calls `push_check`, the port of 0x1AA56 (not while Lomax hangs, flies, rides or walks through a door; the Apple (big) uses it too, see Apple (big) and App2). Actors update before Player_Update, as Flink's thinks run before his. `rock_after` (from `player_tick`, after Player_Update) is the port of 0x1C31C, and sets Lomax's `PUSH` pose while he pushes on the ground, keeping it running across ticks (Player_Update picks his walk each tick; `rock_before` saves the pose's position). `rock_bounce`, called by grab.c's `strike` after the boss's trunks, turns thrown objects back. A rock that falls off the map's bottom is despawned and comes back at its placement, as after a lost life. Drawn on Flink's draw list 2 (behind Lomax).

## Bridges

Flink's `Bridge` (init 0x27C7E, think 0x27D1C, draw 0x27E5E; no params; VILLAGE: one each in 10-1, 11-1, 12-1 and 15-2) is a rope bridge of 12 planks (record `WOOD`) between two posts that are background. The map has no collision there: the actor is the floor.

- **Standing.** Its box (+0x46) is x − 80 … x + 80; the surface is y + 2 + sag[k], k = (player x − x + 64) × 10 / 45, from the table at 0x27E1C (−2, −1, 0 … 9 … 0, −1, −2, then 0s): it sags up to 9 px in the middle. Left of x − 64 (k < 0) Flink reads the code before the table, which gives no floor; the hitbox starts there.
- **The planks.** Each has a timer (0xFF: untouched) and a drop. The plank under the player is k / 2 − 1 (k 2…25; the ends have none). Where it has fallen there is no floor. Stood on, an untouched plank's timer is set to 30; counting down to 0, the plank starts falling (sound 0x29), its timer counting up to 16 and its drop growing by timer / 4 a frame (up to 4 px a frame), until 0xF0, where it is no longer drawn. Walking across, Lomax is past each plank before it falls; standing still, the one under him falls and he drops through. Fallen planks don't come back while the actor lives (out of Flink's activation window it only waits, 0x27CDC).
- **Drawn.** Plank i (0–11) at (x − 54 + 9 i, one more from i = 6, y − 16 + sag[2 + 2 i] + drop): WOOD's first hardware sprite, top-left there.

Each becomes `Mega Bridge`, a static Lomax moving platform (object 64) built by the converter's bridges block in `convert`: record `MAIN` one plank at the actor's point, hitbox x − 65 … x + 81, y1 2 (Lomax's test is strict). FLINK.DLL's `dll\bridge.c` (`bridge_spawn` from `mega_spawn`) steps the planks as Flink's think, calls `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` with `bridge_surface`'s sag (with player state 0x20000 set around it while Lomax is over a fallen plank, as the Wall platforms) and draws each plank with one `Actor_Draw`. Flink's 0x29 is Lomax's WOODBNC1 (effect 54). Enemies and thrown objects fall through the bridge, as in Flink (they stand on map collision only). Unlike Flink, the planks come back when Lomax's cull despawns the bridge and its placement spawns it again, or after a lost life (as the falling platforms).

## Jumppads

Flink's Jumppad is the `Jumppad` actor (0x2BF8E, think 0x2BFBC; no params). It is not Lomax's bounce pad, which throws Lomax at a fixed speed. The converted maps have 28 Jumppads: 10 in FOREST and 18 in VILLAGE. Each world has its own `JPAD` sprites: a leaf on a coil in FOREST, a wooden pad in VILLAGE.

The converter makes each one a `Mega Jumppad n`: a static Mega (object 64) with all of `JPAD`'s frames (0 squashed, 1 at rest, 2 stretched). Its standing surface is not widened, because Flink stands on it only with his centre over its hitbox. FLINK.DLL replaces its update (`jumppad_update`) with Flink's behaviour, at 60 frames a second as Lomax's ticks:
- **Landing on it** keeps Lomax's falling speed and starts a 48-tick timer. Each tick the frame is `{1, 2, 1, 0, 1, 2, 2, 1, 0, 0, 0, 1}[timer / 4]` (table 0x2C0D4): it squashes, stretches and wobbles back to rest. The standing surface moves with the frame (+5, 0, −7 px from the hitbox top, table 0x2C0E0), carrying Lomax (`Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` with a surface function, `jumppad_surface`).
- **While he stands on it** Jump does nothing: the Jumppad zeroes the jump hold force every tick, as Flink's clears its jump boost and as Lomax's bounce pad does.
- **The throw.** 20 ticks after landing (timer 28) the Jumppad plays Lomax's bounce-pad launch sound (0x35, for Flink's 0x2E) and, if he is still on it, throws him up:
  - Jump not held: at the landing speed (at least 4.0) × 100 / 70, at most 7.0. A short hop: 62 px from a 6.0 landing.
  - Jump held at that tick: at the landing speed × 100 / 50, at most 7.0, plus a hold force of 0x7000 that Lomax's own jump code adds while Jump stays held, shrinking by 0x800 a tick. The full jump: 113 px from a 6.0 landing. Letting go of Jump cuts it short.
- He lands on it again at about the speed he left, so he keeps bouncing until he steers off it, as Flink's code does.

Lomax's jump physics are Flink's: gravity 0.375 px a tick up to 6.0, and a jump is 4.0 plus a hold force that shrinks by 0x800 and is added while Jump is held (Lomax's starts at 0x9800, Flink's at 0x9000). So Flink's numbers are used as they are. The throw sets what Lomax's bounce pad sets (airborne and standing-jump state bits, vy and the hold force), except state bit 0x400, which would keep the hold force going after Jump is released. It also clears the take-off delay, as `Player_BounceOffEnemy` does, and the air spin. While Lomax carries a heavy object the throw is at most 5.0 instead of 7.0, as Flink's (see Grabbing and throwing).

### Bouncy platforms

Flink's `Bouncy platform` (init 0x2BE16, think 0x2BE6A; params `x-speed`, checkbox 1 "look right") is TREE's leaf on a stalk: 26, in TREE maps 3 (25-1), 8 (25-2), 4 (26-1), 6 (27-2) and 7 (28-1). It is the Jumppad's throw driven by its `JUMP` animation (29 frames at 182/256 of a frame a tick, played once) instead of a timer, and it throws at a fixed angle. The converter makes each one a `Mega Bouncy n`: a static Mega (object 64) with all of `JUMP`'s frames, mirrored for "look right", the Jumppad's unwidened standing surface, and params 5–7: the signed x speed, `JUMP`'s frame count and its speed. FLINK.DLL replaces its update (`bouncy_update`, `jumppad.c`) with Flink's think, in its order each tick:
- **At rest** it shows `JUMP`'s last frame.
- **Frame 7** plays the launch sound (Lomax's 0x35 for Flink's 0x2E, once: frame 7 lasts one or two ticks) and, if Lomax stood on it the tick before, throws him as the Jumppad does: the landing speed (at least 4.0) × 100 / 70, or × 100 / 50 with the hold force 0x7000 when Jump is held at that tick, at most 7.0 (5.0 carrying a heavy object). It also sets his x speed to its own: `x-speed` × 0x28F (1/100 px a tick), to the left, or to the right with "look right". With `x-speed` 0 (most of them) it throws him straight up and stops his sideways speed; 50, 100 and 200 throw him at 0.5, 1.0 and 2.0 px a tick sideways.
- **The animation** advances and holds on the last frame. The standing surface is the hitbox top + `{0, 2, 4, 7, 10, 4, −9, −15}[frame]` (table 0x2BF7C) for frames 0–7 and the top itself after: the leaf sinks 10 px, springs up 15 px and throws at its highest.
- **Landing on it** keeps Lomax's falling speed and restarts the animation at frame 0, so the throw comes 11 ticks after landing. While he stands on it Jump does nothing (the jump hold force is cleared).

## Seesaws

Flink's seesaw is the `Seesaw` actor (init 0x2BBE2, think 0x2BC0A; no params), a plank on a pivot that works as a seesaw: Flink stands on the low end and throws a heavy object (a big stone, an open chest) onto the high end, which tips the plank and throws him into the air. The converted maps have 8: 2-1 (one, with a big stone next to it), 3-3, 5-1 and 6-1 (two each) and 11-2 (VILLAGE's). Its `SESA` record has 4 frames: 0 the left end down (right end up), 1 and 2 less tilted, 3 the right end down.

The converter makes each one a `Mega Seesaw n` (`plat_seesaw`): a static Mega (object 64) with all of `SESA`'s frames and Flink's hitbox, not widened (as the Jumppad's). FLINK.DLL replaces its update (`seesaw_update`, `dll\seesaw.c`) with Flink's:
- **Tilt.** An 8.8 frame position, 0 to 0x380, that moves by a speed every tick and stops at either end. It starts at 0 each time it spawns.
- **Standing surface.** The hitbox top minus dx × 10 / `{27, 50, −50, −27}`[frame] (table 0x2BD4C), dx being Lomax's x − the seesaw's x: a slope through the pivot (about 27 px from end to end at frames 0 and 3). `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer` carries Lomax on it (`seesaw_surface`).
- **Stood on**, and not throwing, it tilts toward Lomax's side at 0x40 a tick: his end goes down.
- **A thrown object** (one moved by `Actor_ThrownThink` 0x323C2: thrown, dropped or lying at rest; not a shot), falling, inside its hitbox raised 10 px at the top (0x2BD54), on the raised end (left of the pivot with frame ≥ 2, right of it with frame < 2), starts the throw: the plank turns toward that end at 0x80 a tick (7 ticks end to end) and doesn't look for objects again for 60 ticks. Any thrown object counts, heavy or light (thrown straight up). The object falls on through the plank to the ground below it, as in Flink (`grab_falling_on` in grab.c).
- **The throw.** When the plank reaches the end, if Lomax stands on it, he is thrown 9.0 px a tick up (7.0 if he carries a heavy object) and 3.0 sideways toward the end that went down, back over the pivot. Like the Jumppad's throw it sets the airborne bits and clears the jump hold force (Flink's jump boost), the take-off delay and the air spin. Lomax keeps that sideways speed in the air (as Flink's player physics do) unless he steers. Lomax's bounce-pad launch sound (0x35) plays for Flink's 0x34.

## Enemies

Flink's enemies become Lomax objects that draw Flink's own sprites (as the platforms do) and get Flink's behaviour from FLINK.DLL. They are converted one kind at a time; so far the Forest Dummy, the City dummy, the Mountain dummy, the Intree dummy, the Meta dummy, the Storm Cloud, the Blitz dude, the Circling suckers, the Worm, the Yeti, the Buzzard, the Razorbacks (Razorback), the Cyclin' Dude, his Chopper Bike and the Little Dude, the Firemonster, the Ingredient Holder, the Thief, the Visje, the Statue, the Raketen wurm, the Puge, the first two bosses, TREE's, the Donkey monkey, META's, the Meta Boss, and the final boss, MOUNTAIN's Bos1, with his Bos3 dudes.

| Flink | Lomax | How |
|---|---|---|
| Forest Dummy (0x2F948), the basic FOREST enemy: 23 in the converted maps, 4 in level 1-1 | `Muta Dummy n`: object 74 (`Muta`, ZombieRunner) | Its records `WALK`, `STUN`, `SL45` and `DEAD`, with Flink's hitboxes. Param 0 = 1 when Flink's "move left" box is set: it starts facing left. FLINK.DLL wraps object 74's spawn and runs Flink's behaviour (below). |
| City dummy (0x2FBD6), VILLAGE's soldier with a lance: 38 in the converted maps (9-1, 9-2, 10-1 to 10-4, 11-2, 12-1, 13-1, 15-2, 16-1), 6 in level 9-1 | `Muta City n`: object 74 | Its records `STAY`, `WALK`, `ATCK`, `STUN`, `SL45`, `DEAD` and `LANS` (the lance alone, flying off), with Flink's hitboxes, and MAIN.WLD's `Shot` `SPIN`, which it flies as when thrown as a shot (as the Forest Dummy; without it a thrown one slid along in its `STUN` frame). Params: facing left, then its lance tip, `ATCK`'s second box (11, −23, 22, −12 from its feet; the converter's `city_params`, from `Records.box2`: Flink's records carry a second box at bytes 8–11, which 0x1A41C tests). |
| Mountain dummy (0x2FE30), MOUNTAIN's short soldier with a shield: 19 in the converted maps (18-1 three, 18-2 four, 19-2 three, 22-1 three, 22-2's side room five, all spawned by Generators, 24-1 one) | `Muta Moun n`: object 74 | Its records `STAY`, `WALK`, `STUN`, `DEAD` and `SHLD` (the shield alone, flying off), with Flink's hitboxes, and MAIN.WLD's `Shot` `SPIN` (thrown as a shot, as the Forest Dummy). Param: facing left. |
| Intree dummy (0x2FA20), TREE's small beetle: 50 in the converted maps (25-1 seventeen, 25-2 seven, 26-1 eleven, 27-1 two, 27-2 twelve, 28-1 one), 15 of them spawned by Generators | `Muta Intr n`: object 74 | Its records `WALK`, `JUMP`, `FLY ` (gliding), `STUN`, `SL45` and `DEAD`, with Flink's hitboxes, and MAIN.WLD's `Shot` `SMAL` as `SHSM` (it is small, as the Little Dude). Param: facing left. |
| Worm (0x28A1C), TREE's red worm: 7 in the converted maps (25-1 six, 4 of them spawned by Generators; 25-2 one) | `Muta Worm n`: object 74 | Its records `DEAD` (the whole worm: the actor's record, for its hitbox and its death), `HEAD` and `BODY` (2 frames each, one sprite), which FLINK.DLL draws at its segments' points. Param: facing left (Flink's checkbox bit 0 faces it right; no placement has it, so all face left). |
