- **Hits.** Only what is thrown or rolls at it: a thrown object, a shot (which it also turns back, 0x221FA: mirrored, vx negated, moved 4 × that) or a rolling or falling App2. Flink's slide hits it too; Lomax's roll doesn't (nothing of Lomax's hits it). Not while Flink dies, while it is guarded or hurt. A hit: a spirit at y − 70, sound 0x2B, 30 frames guarded (blinking, harmless); standing, it hops in place (2.0 up). Flink's spell 0x31A1E and the spirits don't count. 8 hits (0x8284). It can't be stomped: touching it hurts (0x1A46E), landing on it, spinning or rolling into it too.
- **Its own touch** (0x1A5A0, every frame): the first App2 or Falling apple its box meets is hit, so it breaks an App2 it lands on or jumps through.
- **Defeat.** The fanfare (index 21) plays once; for 161 frames it hops across in `OUCH`, blinking, a spirit every 32 frames (Flink also flashes the screen: not here), and Flink can't be hurt meanwhile (hurt timer −2, as here: see the Forest Boss). Then its floor goes 400 px below the map, it falls out of sight, counts as defeated, and 100 ticks later the Finish appears in the middle of Flink's screen with a poof (Flink drops item 0x19 instead). Defeated, it stays gone for the level visit: the arena then has the Finish from the start.
- **The arena's edges.** While the x scrolls, Flink stays 26–294 px inside its screen (0x1CCCC), so in the fight he is held at x 346–614. Lomax+'s view (432 px at the default widescreen) stops at the map's width − its own and would let Lomax stand left of the monkey's reach and of the apples' places, which Flink's camera never shows. `donk_after` (after Player_Update) holds Lomax at Flink's 26–294 of the map's last 320 px from the fight's first jump until the monkey falls away: an unseen wall at x 346, the floor going on beyond it. The apples' places are on that screen too, so the fight is Flink's at any width.
- **Sounds.** The Forest Boss's landing (EXPLOS2 low, 0x58) for 0x35, the enemy hit for 0x2B, ZAP2 for 0x1A.

**How FLINK.DLL hooks it.** Lomax's `Player_Update` checks every actor flagged 0x20 (hurts) against Lomax in a loop (0x40FEDD). For one also flagged 0x10000 (hittable), when Lomax falls onto its hitbox from above, the loop calls `Player_BounceOffEnemy` (0x410092); otherwise it calls `Player_TakeHit` (0x41009A). The actor itself is never told it was stomped. FLINK.DLL sends both calls through small stubs that pass the actor's slot (at `[esp+0x30]` in the loop) to its own code: a bounce stuns or kills a dummy, and a stunned dummy doesn't hurt. Other actors get the original calls.

**When they start.** Flink starts a placement's actor once its point is in its activation window 0 (0x32AC2, sizes at 0x44148): the camera − 64 … + 384 across (its 320-px screen with 64 px each side), − 48 … + 304 down. Flink's camera x is the player's x − 0xA4 (0x34D52) within the map, unless a Scroll Point moves or holds it. Lomax spawns placements as far as 80–340 px off its screen (`Objects_SpawnNearCamera` 0x412560, the margins at 0x464540 by spawn phase), and Lomax+'s view is wider than Flink's. So enemies would start earlier than Flink's, and several close together on the same tick: 3-2's three Visjes at x 377, 389 and 401 would swim in a bunch 12 px apart, jumping together, where in Flink the scrolling screen reaches them about 45 and 80 frames apart, so they swim about 30 px apart and jump in turn. A Flink enemy that Lomax spawns by proximity (not a Generator's) outside that window waits, its update not run (`enemy_wait`, enemy.c), until the window reaches it. The window uses Flink's camera x as above (Lomax's while a Scroll Point owns it) and Lomax's camera y. While it waits off Lomax's screen (the plane's width − `nMaxScrollX`, i.e. the view Lomax+ sets), it is hidden and harmless. On screen (a wider view shows it before Flink's window reaches it) it is drawn, animated in place and touchable.

## Spirits

Flink's `Spirit` (0x2EDAA, think 0x2EE2C, MAIN.WLD) is set free in Flink by the boss and by the spells; each one Flink touches adds one to `g_MagicPower` (0x82E6, 0–31), the magic shown by the HUD's glass. Here **every defeated enemy** sets one free, from the middle of its hitbox (`enemy_defeated`), as does the boss at each hit (forestboss.c), and a chest that holds a magic key sets 10 free (see Chests). As in Flink, a spirit starts up at 2.0 and homes in on Lomax's x, y − 15 (`Actor_MoveToward` 0x1B008: 0x800 a frame up to 2.0), dropping a trail of 3 `TAIL` puffs; it lives 600 frames, blinking the last 60; touching Lomax it is collected (Lomax's PICKUP4 for Flink's sound 0x1D), then plays `TAIL` once and goes.

- **The count.** `g_spirits` in FLINK.DLL, 0–99: it never goes over 99. It lasts for the whole game, through lives lost and levels, and a new game clears it with Lomax's counters: `Game_ResetNewGame`'s `mov [g_LemmingsFreed], eax` (0x40EE0C, 5 bytes, checked) is a call to `spirits_reset_stub`, which makes that store and clears `g_spirits`. A memory tool finds it through that stub.
- **Hurt.** A hit that knocks Lomax's helmet off costs 5 spirits, down to 0 (`HURT_SPIRITS`; not Flink's); a hit that kills him, like any other death, costs none. `spirits_hurt_after`, after `Player_Update` (`player_tick`), sees `g_PlayerHurtTimer` (0x59E938) go up from 0 to at most 180: `Player_TakeHit` (0x412E30) hits only while it is 0 and sets it to 180 (helmet lost) or 1000 (killed). The knock-back's landing, raising it from below 0, and `boss_untouchable`'s −2 are no new hit. The spirits lost (5, or fewer if he has fewer) fly out of Lomax's middle (`spirits_lose`), fanned out as a chest's (−1.5 to 1.5 px, up at 2.0 or 3.0 px), already blinking as a spirit in its last 60 frames; they don't home in and can't be collected, slow down by 1/24 a frame (`LOST_DRAG`) and after the 60 frames play `TAIL` and go.
- **The currency.** Spirits are Flink's magic: they pay for the spells (see Spells). In Flink each spirit collected adds one to `g_MagicPower` too.
- **The magic glass.** In Flink's maps, where the chosen power's icon and ammo were (top left; powers.c hides them), the HUD shows Flink's glass (MAIN.WLD's `Display` `BOTT`) filling up with the spirits held, and their number next to it in Lomax's big digits (`GROS`, as the lives), left-aligned, the tens only from 10. Flink's `Hud_UpdateMagic` (0x34348) fills the glass from the bottom: power + 1 of its 32 rows come from a full copy made at 0x343E0 by OR-ing colour 0xA (red, palette line 1) into the empty one's pixels under a 1-bit mask at 0x407C8 (32 × 32, 4 bytes a row). The converter (`glass_frames`) makes a frame for each of the mask's 29 rows with bits, frame k filling the lowest k; FLINK.DLL shows frame ⌈spirits × 29 / 99⌉, so 1 spirit shows a little and only 99 fill it. It is drawn by `Hud_Draw`'s call of `Actor_Draw` for the ammo's ones (0x42A6C1, made every tick; powers.c), with the HUD actor given the glass's type for that call, the digits queued first so that they are in front of the glass (within a draw bucket the first sprite queued is in front), at fixed offsets from the ammo's x and y, which Lomax+ moves for widescreen and the 50 Hz layout. The glass flickers as Flink's gauge does (Flink's HUD draw at 0x333E2), its count never: with no spirits it shows 16 ticks and hides 16 (bit 4 of the tick count; Flink's frame count 0x8710), and for 180 ticks (3 seconds) after a hit that costs spirits it shows 8 ticks and hides 8 (bit 3 of `g_glassFlicker`, Flink's `g_NoMagicFlashTimer` 0x830E, which Flink sets for 64 frames when a spell can't be paid and clears at 0 spirits).
- **Converter.** Every map gets the types `Spik Spirit` (`SPIR`, `TAIL`) and `Spik Glass` (`BOTT`, 30 frames), never placed; FLINK.DLL finds them by name (`ObjType_FindByName` 0x4162D0). A map converted before them has no spirits and no glass, and the count is still drawn. FLINK.DLL: `dll\spirits.c`.

## Spells

In Flink the chosen spell (`g_SelectedSpell` 0x6396, −1 none) is picked in the pause menu among the spells brewed (`PauseMenu_SelectSpell` 0x16F7E), cast with the spell button and paid with magic (`g_MagicPower`); the HUD shows its icon, MAIN.WLD's `Available Ingredients` `INGR` frame spell + 30 (0x33FFA). A spell whose cost is not below the magic held only makes the HUD flash for 64 frames (`g_NoMagicFlashTimer` 0x830E). The MAGIC KEY (spell 11) is never cast: with it chosen, a stomp opens a locked chest and uses up a key (0x2E196).

Here Lomax chooses Flink's spells on his own power wheel and pays them with the spirits held (see Spirits). The spells he has, in the wheel's order (`SPELLS` in spells.c; more can be added there):

| Spell | Cost | Used on |
|---|---|---|
| SPIRIT BOMB (0) | 4 spirits a spirit sent (Flink's `Spell0_HomingSpirits` 0x30C66: 4 each) | anywhere: Ability (see Spells cast anywhere) |
| DUST DEVIL (1) | 8 spirits (Flink's `Spell1_Tornado` 0x30F12: 8) | anywhere: Ability |
| LIGHTNING (2) | 10 spirits (Flink's `Spell2_Lightning` 0x31CC6: 10) | anywhere: Ability |
| QUICK GROW (4) | 4 spirits (Flink's `Spell4_GrowPlant` 0x315C2: 4 magic) | a Plant Lomax stands on: Ability (see Plant) |
| GHOST (6) | 8 spirits (Flink's `Spell6_KillAreaWarp` 0x3227E: 8) | 20-1's altar: Ability, the shortcut to level 29 (see Shortcuts) |
| SHRINK (7) | 8 spirits (Flink's `Spell7_Shrink` 0x31F1E: 8) | 14-1's tree: Ability, the shortcut to level 25 (see Shortcuts) |
| PLATFORM (8) | 8 spirits (Flink's `Spell8_CloudPlatform` 0x3140E: 8) | anywhere: Ability |
| SHIELD (9) | 10 spirits (Flink's `Spell9_OrbitingSpirits` 0x31606: 10) | anywhere: Ability |
| DEMON (10) | 8 spirits to summon its demon, 6 to grow it (Flink's `Spell10_Demon` 0x31856: 8, then 6); the HUD shows 6 while the demon can grow | anywhere: Ability |
| MAGIC KEY (11) | 10 spirits (Flink's costs a key, no magic; Lomax has no keys, and a chest's magic key gives 10 spirits) | a locked chest Lomax stands at: Ability (see Chests) |
| HELMET (12, not Flink's) | 5 spirits | anywhere: Ability (see HELMET) |

The costs are Flink's, the MAGIC KEY's excepted; HELMET is Lomax's own.

- **The wheel.** Select opens it, as Lomax's (`Player_Update` calls `PowerWheel_Open` 0x430BA0 at 0x40FCD6; in Flink's maps powers.c calls `spell_wheel_open` instead). It works as Lomax's (`PowerWheel_Update` 0x430C90, `PowerWheel_Draw` 0x430FA0) for any number of icons: it opens around Lomax, the radius growing by 2 px a tick to 60 while it turns 6° a tick (half a turn), Lomax frozen (player state 0x80), sound 0x17, the CD paused on a level of world 5; holding Left picks the next spell and Right the one before (sound 0x41), the wheel turning by the gap between two icons in 15 ticks; Select closes it (sound 0x18). While it is open the other actors are frozen; on the tick it closes, the platform Lomax stands on, if its slot comes before the wheel's (skipped that tick), is updated at once, or `Player_Update` would drop him onto the map's ground under it (a Plant). The chosen spell is the icon at the top, drawn whole, the others translucent (alpha 0xA0). The icons are Flink's own, with no black square behind them, HELMET's excepted (see HELMET). With eleven icons the gap between two is 360/11°: the angles are counted in 15ths of a gap (`wheel_angle`). Ability stays the grab/throw button.
- **The HUD.** Right of the magic glass and its count: the chosen spell's icon, and next to it its cost in Lomax's small digits (`KLEI`, as the coins and the defeated enemies). A spell is paid with its cost or more held. A cost that can't be paid makes the digits flicker red for 120 ticks (about 2 seconds), 8 ticks red and 8 white in turn. The press also plays Lomax's GRAB1 (sound 14, `SND_NO_SPIRITS`; Flink plays none). Drawn at fixed offsets from the ammo's position, as the glass (spirits.c's `spirits_hud_draw` calls `spells_hud_draw`). Each of these pieces, and the glass and its count, is drawn through a stand-in of its own, a copy of the HUD actor (`hud_piece` in spirits.c): Lomax+'s FPS Override matches a sprite with the last tick's by actor and n-th `Actor_Draw` call of that actor, and `Hud_Draw` draws a varying number of pieces before these (the tens of its counts, the spare helmet), as do the tens here. Drawn as the HUD actor's n-th call, a piece would be matched with a neighbour whenever such a count changed (choosing a spell with a two-digit cost, say) and drawn sliding from it on the in-between frames for a tick. A ones digit has a stand-in for each layout (with and without its tens). HELMET's icon, of another type and centred by its own offset, has a stand-in apart from the Flink spells' icons, whose anchor it would otherwise move when the choice changes.
- **Casting.** A spell is cast with Ability, the grab button (Lomax has no spell button), on the ground and not while hurt, as Flink's spell button (`Player_HandleButtons` 0x32E0E; `spell_can_cast`). Not Flink's: during a boss fight only HELMET is cast. Each boss's update calls `boss_fighting` (forestboss.c) every tick from its fight's start (the Forest Boss dropping in, the Village Boss running in, the Meta Boss rising, the final boss's first fireball, the Donkey monkey's first jump) until it is gone, and `boss_fight` holds two ticks after the last call: meanwhile Select still opens the wheel (`spell_wheel_open`), every other spell is refused, HELMET's helmet comes as anywhere else, and the spells already out hit nothing (`spell_strike`). The press goes first to the object it is for (a Plant Lomax stands on, a locked chest within reach, GHOST's or SHRINK's area), then to a pick-up; with nothing in reach it casts the spells cast anywhere (`spell_cast`, from grab.c's `grab_before`). Carrying something, it throws.
- **The chosen spell** stays through lives lost, levels and new games. The cost check and payment is `spell_pay`, the selection test `spell_chosen`.
- **Shortcuts.** GHOST and SHRINK work only in their areas, Flink's `Kill area` (init 0x22C18, think 0x22C54) and `Shrink area` (init 0x22CCE, think 0x22D28), whose thinks set `g_InKillArea` (0x825F) / `g_InShrinkArea` (0x825D) each frame while the player's y is the area's and his x within 30 px of it: 14-1's tree (L31, Shrink area at 1304, 240) and 20-1's altar (L44, Kill area at 671, 208). In Flink SHRINK takes Flink into the tree (TREE, world 4) and GHOST out of his body into META (world 5). Here each is a shortcut to a level that the play order reaches later (see Levels, Play order): SHRINK goes on to level 25, GHOST to level 29: the screen fades out to the world map, which leaves for that level (`g_LastRealSection` set as the level-complete banner sets it, the lives kept; `level_shortcut` in levels.c). A shortcut is taken once a game (`g_shortcuts`, bits 1 SHRINK and 2 GHOST): with it taken the area takes no spell and the press is a pick-up. Taken, it changes the play order: after SHRINK, finishing 28 leads back to 14 (its first sub-level) instead of 18, and finishing 16 leads to 18 instead of 25; after GHOST, finishing 31 leads back to 20 instead of 23, and finishing 22 leads to 23 instead of 29 (`DETOURS`). Coming back to 14 or 20, the world map shows the change its area makes (the tree's base, the path over the clouds; `level_map_rank`). The shortcuts last through lives lost and continues; going back to the title clears them.
  - **SHRINK.** Flink's `Spell7_Shrink` scales Flink down over 100 steps while he can't be hurt, the music fading at step 42 and the level left below step 10. Here Lomax stops (the input locked, as at an Exit) and shrinks around his feet from 100% to 10% over 100 ticks, drawn as he is in a door (`g_PlaneTransitionScale` not 0 keeps him from being hurt; `player_draw`, `spell_scale`), the music fading 42 ticks before the end and the screen fading out 10 ticks before it.
  - **GHOST.** Flink's `Spell6_KillAreaWarp` shows a puff at cast frame -30 and leaves the level on its 3rd frame. Here Lomax leaves as into a Finish trapdoor (`Trapdoor_Update` 0x42AD80: player state | 0x43, the input locked, vy -4.0 and then 0x7000 more upward a tick, the jump animation `Player_Update` gives that state; 0x40 keeps the camera's y), with no trapdoor to clip him: he jumps up off the top of the screen, which fades out from tick 20. His hurt timer is held at -1 as the trapdoor holds it, and the blink Lomax gets for a negative timer (his +0x78) is cleared every tick, or he would not be drawn.
  - **The password** keeps the shortcuts. Lomax's password is 16 bits (`Map_DrawPasswordGems` 0x4243C0, `Password_DecodeAndApply` 0x417950): v = code ^ 0xB7A4 holds a 4-bit checksum, the section (5 bits), the lives (5) and the continues (2), no bit free. The shortcut bits go into the checksum's two low bits: the code is XORed with 0xB7A4 ^ `g_shortcuts`. A password without shortcuts is Lomax's own; the checksum keeps its two high bits. FLINK.DLL patches the draw's key (the immediate at 0x424464, `password_sync`, whenever the shortcuts change) and wraps the decode (Menu_OnEnterPassword's call at 0x40E703): the checksum's difference from the one the bits give is the shortcuts (more than 3: a bad password), the decode's key (0x417968) is set to match for the call, and its call of `Game_ResetNewGame` (0x417A43), made only when the player confirms, sets them. FLINK.DLL: `dll\password.c`.
  - **Converter.** LEVELS.INI's `[Areas]` lists the areas, `FILE=type,x,y` (`SPELL_AREAS`); they aren't placed as objects.
- **HELMET** (spell 12, `SPELL_HELMET`; not Flink's). Cast anywhere with Ability (`spell_cast` calls spells.c's `spell_helmet_cast`), it gives Lomax what a picked-up helmet gives him (`Item_UpdateCoinOrHelmet` 0x42C6A0): the helmet (`g_bHasHelmet` 0x52C570) if he wears none and has none thrown (`g_bHelmetInFlight` 0x59E944), else the spare one (`g_bHasSpareHelmet` 0x59F1E4), paid 5 on the press, with the pickup's sound (0x14, FANFARE; the pickup's sparkles are not shown). With both it does nothing and costs nothing. Its icon is Lomax's own: a frame of the `Items` type's `HELM` record in Lomax's MAIN.WLD (bank 35), `HELMET_ICON_FRAME` in spells.c, drawn through that type (with MAIN.WLD's palette) on the wheel and the HUD, the box of the frame's pieces centred on the icon's point (`icon_set`).
- **Spells cast anywhere** (`dll\spellfx.c`). Flink's handlers (`SpellHandlerTable` 0x32F0A) stop Flink and start the cast gesture, the timer 0x830C that `Player_Update` counts while Flink stands (0x1CBA2): `MAG1` down to 0, then, unless `g_CastNoFollowThrough` (0x8310), `MAG2` from −95 up to 0, sound 0x20 at −40. Walking holds the count, leaving the ground ends it (0x1C7E2). Lomax has no casting pose, so nothing waits for it: PLATFORM, SHIELD and DEMON, which Flink holds until −30 (85 ticks after the press), take effect on the press with the cast sound (`cast_now`), as QUICK GROW, GHOST and SHRINK do. SPIRIT BOMB, DUST DEVIL and LIGHTNING act on the press in Flink too; their count (no follow-through) still runs (`cast_tick`, walking holding it): it keeps another spell from being cast meanwhile and paces SPIRIT BOMB's spirits. Their actors are MAIN.WLD's, spawned by code (`Actor_SpawnFromNmy` 0x32BE0), and they hit as a thrown object does (they set the hit flag, 0x1A6D2): `spell_strike` in grab.c, the thrown object's test. Flink's screen flash (main command 0x28, for SPIRIT BOMB and LIGHTNING) has no Lomax counterpart. Sounds: Lomax's ICONSLCT for the gesture (0x20), ZOOM1 for SPIRIT BOMB (0x2B), SPIN02 for DUST DEVIL (0x2C), EXPLOS1 for LIGHTNING (0x2D), ICONOUT for the cloud's puffs (0x42); DEMON has no sound of its own.
  - **SPIRIT BOMB.** Not while one of its spirits flies. A spirit (MAIN.WLD's `Spirit`, `SPIR`) for each enemy on the screen, one enemy each (`g_SpiritTargetMask` 0x839C), 4 spirits each while 4 or more are held: out of Lomax at y − 20 at 4.0 px up (0x30CD4); gesture 0x4F, no follow-through. Each spirit sends the next one out once, when the gesture's count & 15 is 0 (0x30E50): one every 16 ticks while the gesture lasts, then one a tick. A spirit homes in on its enemy's middle (`Actor_MoveToward` 0x1B008: up to 2.5 px, 0x3000 a tick), hits it on touching it and plays `TAIL` (0x2EEEC); its enemy gone, off the screen or no longer stompable, it becomes a spirit set free (0x2EE2C, spirits.c), homing in on Lomax for 1200 ticks and collected for 1.
  - **DUST DEVIL.** Not while a Tornado blows. Four Tornados (MAIN.WLD's `Tornado`, `DUST`) start 0x40 px apart from 0x40 px past the screen's right edge, 64..191 px above it, each with a top speed of 2.0..2.875 px, a height on the screen of 32..159 px and four blades (`BLAD`, frames 0/1 and 2/3 by turns every 16 ticks, the last two mirrored) flying off left; sound 0x2C once on the screen; gesture 0x3C, no follow-through. A Tornado (0x31142) takes the first enemy on the screen not yet taken (`g_TornadoTargetMask` 0x8388; slots of one parity a tick) and flies toward its feet (0xC00 a tick) for up to 300 ticks; touching it hits it, and 120 ticks later it is done; a dying enemy (`Enemy_Die`'s fall 0x1B1AA) it catches, the body riding 8 px above it (0x313E6). With no enemy it flies toward 140 px of Flink's 320 from the screen's left at its height (0x800 a tick), done once past it. Done, it flies toward 200 px left of the screen (0x800 a tick) and goes, with its body, once off the screen. The x's of Flink's 320-px screen are scaled to Lomax's view.
  - **LIGHTNING.** Not while a bolt strikes. Five bolts (MAIN.WLD's `Lightning`), one after the other (0x31D36, 0x31D7C): each tries 10 ticks for a place, x 32..287 px into the screen (scaled to the view), on the ground below a random point of the screen's top 128 px (`Map_GetGroundHeight` 0x1AEEA, 20 cells: Lomax's `Plane_FindGroundY`) within the screen (the 10th try: 96 px lower, no ground), its point 62 px above, unseen meanwhile. Then `FLA1` once; `FLA2` once, sound 0x2D and the shake (the camera's counter 0x874E = 0x23 when still; razorback.c's), hitting the first actor its hitbox touches, below the bolt's point, each tick (`Actor_FindTouchingActor` 0x1A5A0); `FLA3` once, the next bolt starting with it. Gesture 0x3C, no follow-through.
  - **PLATFORM.** A cloud (MAIN.WLD's `Platform`, `WOLK`; Flink's waits unseen for the gesture's −30, 0x31474) is paid on the press (or the cost flashes) and placed 40 px in front of Lomax and 20 px up, with Lomax's poof 8 px lower (Flink's `Puff` `PLOF`) and sound 0x42. For 480 ticks (0x31508): the first 15 unseen under the puff, `WARN` from 60 ticks left, then `PUFF` once (sound 0x42) and gone. It is a platform (0x1A28A: `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer`, its speed in the actor only for the call, as the Plant's): landed on, it dips 0.5 px a tick for 15 ticks and comes back up at 0.5 px to its height; left before, it comes back at once. Any number of clouds.
  - **SHIELD.** Not while its spirits circle. Three spirits (`SPIR`; Flink's wait for the gesture's −30, 0x31726) are paid on the press (or the cost flashes) and circle Lomax (0x31772), 120° apart, 10/1024 of a turn a tick around his x, y − 20, the radius growing 1 px a tick to 31 (27 up and down), with the spirits' trail. Each hits the first actor it touches and goes (`TAIL`). Their count (`g_OrbitSpiritCount` 0x814E, `g_shieldCount`) comes back circling at full radius when a map starts (`Spell9_RestoreOrbitSpirits` 0x316F0) and is lost when Lomax dies (`Player_Die`), at the title and on a new game. In Flink a gesture stopped before −30 frees the waiting spirits but keeps their count (a restart brings them back unpaid). Flink's spirit touching a Rock or an actor whose timer (+0x38) runs stops it instead of going; here a Rock is not something a spell hits, and the timer is not looked at.
  - **DEMON.** A demon (MAIN.WLD's `Demon`) that follows Lomax and attacks the enemies near him; its hits left are `g_DemonActive` (0x814C, `g_demonHits`).
    - *Summoning* (no demon): Flink's demon waits unseen for the gesture's −30 (0x318E0, think 0x31992); here, on the press, it is paid 8 (or the cost flashes and it goes) and appears 30 px in front of Lomax and 15 px up, small (`FLY1`), with 5 hits and a sparkle (0x2ECA6).
    - *Growing* (its demon there, small): on the press (Flink's −30), 6 is paid (or flashes) and the demon grows (0x31A3A): 5 hits more and the horned `FLY2` / `ATK2` for `FLY1` / `ATK1` (`g_DemonGrown` 0x814B), with a sparkle. Grown, the press does nothing (Flink's handler returns).
    - *Fighting* (think 0x31A1E): the small demon fights as the grown one; only its look and hits differ. It takes the first standing enemy (not a Rock) within 80 px of Lomax's x and 60 px of his y (the enemy's y + 20), one slot parity a tick, and flies at it (`Actor_MoveToward` 0x1B008: up to 4.0 px, 0x4000 a tick) in `ATK`, drawn in front of Lomax, while the enemy stands, is the same one and is within 100 px; touching it hits it (0x1A65A, 0x1A6D2; here `spell_strike`) and it rests 90 ticks. Otherwise it flies in `FLY`, behind Lomax, toward 30 px behind him and 35 px up (up to 2.5 px, 0x2000 a tick). `FLY` and `ATK` keep the animation's frame; it turns at more than 1.0 px a tick. Its hits run out: it sparkles and goes (0x31CBA, 120 ticks unseen).
    - *Kept*: it comes back when a map starts (`Spell10_RestoreDemon` 0x3193A: 30 px left of Lomax and 35 px up, its hits and form), and is lost when Lomax dies (`Player_Die`), at the title and on a new game.
    - *Changed from Flink*: Flink's spells 1, 2, 8 and 10 share one graphics slot (`Spell_LoadGraphics` 0x344A4), so choosing DUST DEVIL, LIGHTNING or PLATFORM loses the demon (0x814A); Lomax keeps it. Flink keeps the hits of a summon that can't be paid at −30 (the next map brings the demon unpaid); here it doesn't.
    - *The sparkle* (0x2ECA6, `spirit_sparkle` in spirits.c): an unseen `Spirit` whose draw (0x2ECFC) puts three `TAIL` puffs at random over its actor (x − 11 + 0..7, y − 23 + 0..24), each starting again when its count (0x30 a tick) reaches 0x400, five in all.
- **Converter.** Every map gets the type `Spik Spells`, never placed: `SPEL` (frame n is spell n's icon, `INGR` frame 30 + n, centred on the actor's point), `KLEI` (Lomax's small digits from his `WORLDS\MAIN.WLD`, `Display` `KLEI`, with their 8 greys tinted red: red + 32, green and blue halved, put into palette 0's free entries after the template sprites' colours: `red_digits`), and MAIN.WLD's `Lightning` `FLA1`, `FLA2`, `FLA3`, `Tornado` `DUST`, `BLAD` (`BLAD_FRAMES` frames of one piece, checked) and `Demon` `FLY1`, `ATK1`, `FLY2`, `ATK2`. It also gets `Spik Cloud`: MAIN.WLD's `Platform` `WOLK` as its `MAIN` record (Lomax's standing surface is the `MAIN` record's hitbox, widened by `PLATFORM_EDGE` as the platforms'), `WARN` and `PUFF` (`cloud_type`). A map converted before it has no wheel (Select does nothing), no spell on the HUD and no spells cast anywhere. FLINK.DLL: `dll\spells.c`, `dll\spellfx.c`.

## Chests

Flink's treasure chests (`Chest`, 0x2E000; MAIN.WLD's `Chest` type) keep Flink's sprites and behaviour, with Lomax's pot contents inside. Flink's 62 maps place 152 chests, all in the converted maps: 138 become chests (10 of them locked) and 14 become hint hats.

| Flink's item (the chest's param, `Item_CheckCollect` 0x2C4FE) | Lomax | Chests |
|---|---|---|
| 0, an extra life | a 1UP balloon (`Item_UpdateOneUp`) | 33 |
| 1, a magic key | 10 spirits (`CHEST_KEY_SPIRITS`), set free from the chest's top and homing on Lomax (what a locked chest costs) | 9 |
| 2–4, a small potion (all are 3) | 5 spirits (`CHEST_ITEM_SPIRITS`), set free as a magic key's | 32 |
| 5–6, a large potion (all are 5) | 5 spirits | 15 |
| 7–19, an ingredient | 5 coins (`CHEST_COINS`), thrown as from a Lomax pot | 45 |
| −100 − n, the recipe scroll for spell n (SPIRIT BOMB, DUST DEVIL, QUICK GROW, DEMON) | 5 spirits | 4 |
| −1 … −99, an information note (Flink's message −id − 1) | no chest: a Lomax hint hat (`Lorock`, object 69) 40 px above the chest's floor, whose hint box shows Flink's message | 14 |

The ingredients: Eagle's feather 5, Plant 3, Gold ring 7, Silver ring 6, Diamond ring 4, Magic mirror 1, Magic necklace 3, Magic amulet 3, Skull 2, Tiger's eye 2, Magic root 3, Goblin's tooth 2, Fairy tear 4. The magic keys are in 3-4, 4-1, 4-2, 5-2, 7-1 (two), 11-2, 16-1 and 18-1.

**The chest** is a `Pots Chest` object (object 85, Lomax's clay pot, whose spawn FLINK.DLL wraps) with Flink's records `CHES` and `OPEN` and the clods `ERDE` (MAIN.WLD's `Dustcloud`). Its param is the content, as a Lomax pot's (1 1UP, −n coins; no chest holds Lomax's 0, a helmet), or 100 + n for n spirits (`CHEST_SPIRITS`, in both the converter and chests.c; Lomax's pots have no such content, so only FLINK.DLL's `chest_drop` knows it). FLINK.DLL (`chest_update`) gives it Flink's behaviour, at 60 frames a second as Lomax's ticks:
- **Bursting out of the ground.** Every time it spawns, a Flink chest starts 16 px under its placement, hidden behind the ground's tiles, is thrown up at 2.5 px a frame and falls back onto its placement (gravity 0x2000 a frame, 0x2E138). Here Lomax's bottom clip line (actor +0x7C, which Lomax's own pots use when they burst out of the ground) hides the part under the placement. Five clods fly off (`Fx_SpawnDebris` 0x2E0BC, table 0x2E106: a delay of 0–20 frames, an offset and a speed each), each for 60 frames, alternating `ERDE`'s two frames. Lomax's debris sound (0x3D) plays for Flink's 0x49. Most chests are hidden until a Generator spawns them as Lomax comes near (see Hidden objects), so the burst is usually seen, as in Flink.
- **Opening.** Flink opens a chest by stomping on it. Here Lomax's stomp does it (the contact loop's bounce, as for the Forest Dummy), and so do his attacks (spin, tumble, the thrown helmet), which open Lomax's pots. A closed chest doesn't hurt. It shows `OPEN`, plays Lomax's pot-hit sound (0x11) and lets the content out as an opened Lomax pot does (`ClayPot_UpdateOpened` 0x42C2E0): the 1UP rises out of it, the coins are thrown out with the speeds from the table at 0x4645E8. A spirits chest (a magic key's, a potion's or a recipe scroll's) instead sets its spirits free from its top (`spirit_release`, at most 20 from one chest), fanned out from 1.5 px left to 1.5 px right and going up at 2.0 or 3.0 px, then homing on Lomax and collected as a defeated enemy's (see Spirits; the count stops at 99).
- **Opened.** Nothing opens it again: hitting it does nothing (Lomax's pots break at the second hit). The open chest can be picked up and thrown, as in Flink; Lomax has to crouch for it (see Grabbing and throwing).
- **Opened for good.** Flink forgets an opened chest's map entry, as it does for every item taken and every enemy killed (the saved pending bits at 0x6480, 0x1B13A; `Item_Spawn` 0x2C21A). Once the chest leaves the screen it never comes back: not after a lost life, and not when the map is entered again through a door. Without this, the 1UPs in 3-1's secret room (3-4) could be taken again and again.

**Gone for good** (opened chests and dead enemies):
- The placement remembers it (`+0x14` bit 1, `PLACEMENT_GONE`) for the rest of this life: `Level_InitPlanes(0)` (0x40EC40), which `Level_Start` calls on every restart, zeroes every placement's `+0x14`.
- FLINK.DLL also keeps a list for the level (`g_gone`: sub-level and placement x, y), which keeps them gone across lost lives and the map's reload through a door. The list is cleared when the level is entered from the map screen (`world_load`) and when a continue is taken (`init_planes` in levels.c): after a continue every enemy and chest of the level is back.
- Such an object spawns hidden and harmless, and its first update removes it with `Actor_Kill` (`spawn_gone`, `gone_update`). That leaves its placement marked spawned, so neither the camera nor a Generator spawns it again in that map load.
- **Locked chests** (checkbox 1; 10 of them: three in 5-1, one in 5-2, two in 7-1, one in 11-2 and the three on 18-1's upper ledge, MOUNTAIN map 4). In Flink they show a padlock (the spawn keeps `LOCK`'s frame, 0x2E058, which the draw hook 0x2E2B2 draws as a bare sprite with its top-left at x − 10, y − 19, on the chest's front), and a stomp opens one only with the MAGIC KEY spell selected, using up a key (0x2E196); otherwise it plays the locked sound 0x18. Opened, the padlock flies off (0x2E1D6: `LOCK` thrown up at 3.0 px and 1.5 px the way Flink faces). Here a locked chest opens only by **Ability** (the grab button, with which the spells are cast; see Spells), not by a stomp or an attack: Lomax on the ground, not hurt, with the chest's hitbox within 8 px of his (`CHEST_REACH`), presses it (`chest_unlock`, from grab.c's `grab_before` before a pick-up). With the MAGIC KEY chosen on the spell wheel it costs **10 spirits**, Lomax having no keys: with 10 or more the chest opens; with fewer the cost on the HUD flickers red. With another spell chosen the press plays the locked sound (Lomax's ROCKHITX for Flink's 0x18). A stomp on a locked chest plays that sound too and bounces Lomax off it; an attack does nothing, silently (Lomax's spin hits the chest every tick, and a sound would repeat). The padlock is drawn with Flink's `LOCK` record before the chest (within a draw bucket the first queued sprite is in front), moved by the record's piece offset so that the sprite's top-left is where Flink puts it (`lock_origin`); opened, it flies off from there and falls for 120 ticks. Converter: the chest's param 1 (`Pots Chest` has 2 params); FLINK.DLL: `chest_try`, `chest_draw`, `padlock_fly` (chests.c).

**Information notes.** In Flink, touching a note shows a message (`HelpTextTable` 0x18250: English, French, German, Spanish and Dutch). The converter writes the 14 messages that chests hold into `LEVELS.INI`'s `[Hints]` section and gives the hint hat param 1000 + n:
- `n.L` is message n in Lomax's language L: 0 English, 1 French, 2 German, 4 Spanish. Italian falls back to English.
- Its lines are separated by `|`: Flink's rows, with the gaps as blank lines.

Lomax's hint hat drops a hint guy when touched. His text box (`HintText_Draw` 0x433DF0, stored by `HintGuy_UpdateAppear` at 0x433B9E) is drawn by FLINK.DLL's `hint_text_draw`, which prints Flink's lines the same way. Some German lines have 25 characters and Lomax's box holds 24, so those messages get a box 13 cells wide. Lomax's own hint indexes (below 1000) are drawn by the game as before.

## Hidden objects

Much of Flink appears only when Flink gets somewhere: most chests burst out of the ground as he comes near, and secret ones show up only where he must go to find them (in 1-1 the 1UP chest over the sinking platform appears when he stands on that platform; near the end of 1-2 a leaf appears under a blind jump to the left, leading to another 1UP). Enemies, platforms and hazards are spawned the same way. Lomax has the same feature, and the mod uses it.

**Flink.** A `Generator` (0x2DD54) is an unseen actor. When Flink's box (0x82D0–0x82D6) touches x − 16 … x + 16, y − height … y + 16, it spawns the GNMY entry it links to, then the one that entry links to, and so on:
- A linked entry has spawn flag bit 7 (GNMY +0x0A); its last word is the next entry's offset in the list, not a parameter.
- The spawned entries usually have spawn flag bit 5, never spawned by proximity, so they appear only then.
- `height` is param 0 with checkbox 1, else 16.
- Param 1, when not 0, is a flag of the level (0x6310 + 2 × level) that the Generator sets when it fires; it never fires again.
- Without checkbox 3 (`no scr check`), the spawned actors stay while any of them is near the camera, and then go together; with it they despawn one by one.
- After firing, the Generator despawns. When it scrolls in again it can fire again for objects that are not out, unless they were collected or killed for good.

**Lomax.** Its placements have the same links (`World_LoadWLD`: flag 0x2000 never spawned by proximity, 0x8000 linked, followed by the next placement's index in the plane's list). Its trigger, `Gene` (object 86, `Trigger_Spawn` 0x42D0D0 / `Trigger_Update` 0x42D160), spawns such a chain when Lomax's hitbox holds its x and reaches its height. A chain of two or more becomes one group, which `TriggerGroup_UpdateDespawn` (0x42D2B0) despawns once all of it is off screen.

**The conversion.** The converter (`generator_links`) carries every link over and hides every converted object that Flink hides. Each Flink Generator becomes a `Gene Flink` object, linked to the first converted entry of its chain, with params height and mode (1 = once, 2 = no group). Entries that aren't converted are left out of the chains, and a Generator with nothing converted in its chain is left out. The converted maps have 329 Generators and 429 hidden objects (the sum of the 62 maps' summary lines, side rooms included; 5 of each are the Scroll Points that Generators spawn) (most of the Generators left out spawn enemies that aren't converted yet). FLINK.DLL wraps object 86's spawn and gives `Gene Flink` its own update (`gene_update`):
- It fires with Flink's box, against Lomax's hitbox.
- Every object it spawns goes into a group (even a single one), unless mode 2. The group drops members whose slot no longer holds them (killed, or the slot reused) before `TriggerGroup_UpdateDespawn` runs, so it never despawns another actor and never lets a killed one come back.
- The group's member is the live actor of the placement, looked up after `Object_TrySpawn`: Lomax's rope (`Rope_Spawn` 0x429F60) moves itself to a high slot (`Actor_MoveToHighSlot` 0x412B50), so the actor `Object_TrySpawn` returns is not it. Left out of the group, such a rope would be culled by its own phase as soon as it is off screen (15-1's third liana, at (224, 118), is spawned by the Generator at (293, 432) on the floor 300 px below it).
- The group despawns when its members' x leaves the window, whatever their y, as Flink's watcher (0x2DFB0) does: Flink gives the members activation window 0x18, {0, 0xFFFF, 0, 0xFFFF}, which never culls them. FLINK.DLL (`group_update`) puts the members' y at the camera's around its call of `TriggerGroup_UpdateDespawn`, which keeps that function's x test and Lomax+'s widened window.
- The group's window is at least Lomax's cull phase 1 (160 px past each side of the view; `GROUP_PHASE` in generator.c). Flink's watcher keeps the group while its x span is inside the largest of the members' activation windows, and the Swingplatform (init 0x2B414) and the Drop Ball (0x27B38) have Flink's mid window (8: camera − 128 … + 448). Their Lomax objects have phase 0, 80 px, so at width 320 a group placed farther ahead than that was despawned the moment its Generator fired (16-1's last two wheels, x 1049 and 1174 from the Generator at 827, never appeared at 320; at 432 they were just inside). An offline scan of every map's groups (members' Lomax phase: 1 for enemies, 0 otherwise, members that never cull left out) found one more at 320: 10-3's Drop Ball at x 440 (the Generator at 142); none at 432 or 512. 160 px around Lomax's view covers Flink's mid window at every width (Flink's camera is Lomax's x − 0xA4).
- Once fired it stays and fires again (16 ticks later at the earliest, as Flink's init waits) whenever Lomax touches it and its objects are not out, or with mode 1 it is removed. Flink's Generator despawns, but Flink's `Spawn_NearCamera` (0x32B6C) spawns every pending entry inside its window each frame, on screen too, so it is back at once. Lomax spawns an object only as it scrolls in from off screen, so a Generator that despawned on screen would not come back: at width 320 4-2's Trunks (x 1404, 1598, the Generator at 1207) would despawn with their group while Lomax is back at the liana, the Generator in view, and never come back. The `Gene Flink` is hidden, which `Actors_DrawAndCull` never culls, so it despawns itself 160 px off the view (`GENE_NEAR`), past where its placement spawns (80 px; 110 on a rotating plane), and comes back by proximity.

Flink also removes an object that would appear on screen at its first draw, unless the Generator has its `spec. generator` box (the 1-2 leaf). Here objects always appear, since Lomax's view is wider than Flink's 320 px and an object that never appears would be worse than one that pops in. In a wide view an enemy can therefore appear at the edge of the screen (1-1's second dummy, 216 px ahead of its Generator).

## Grabbing and throwing

Flink picks things up and throws them with its ACTION button (`g_BtnGrabBit` 0x8144). Lomax does it with **Ability**, whose helmet powers are off in Flink's maps (see How FLINK.DLL works, Powers). There are no carrying poses yet: Lomax's own animations play, and the object is placed from his hitbox. FLINK.DLL `grab.c` follows Flink's `Player_HandleButtons` (0x32E0E), `Actor_CheckPickedUp` / `Actor_TryPickUp` (0x1ABB4 / 0x1ABDE), the carry hooks (0x1B5B2), `Actor_ThrownThink` (0x323C2) and the shot (0x32630).

**What can be picked up:**

| Object | Kind (Flink's +6 bits) | Lomax picks it up |
|---|---|---|
| A stunned Forest Dummy, City dummy or Mountain dummy (their stun routine 0x1D074) | light, with a fuse: its stun timer | standing or crouching |
| A stunned Little Dude (the same routine) | light, small, with a fuse: its stun timer; held at +4, +4 | crouching |
| A stunned Intree dummy (the same routine) | light, small, with a fuse: its stun timer; held at +6, +6 | crouching |
| The Chopper Bike, let go (0x30754) | light: Lomax rides it (see Enemies) | by touching it, no button |
| An open chest (0x2E250) | heavy | crouching |
| A big stone (`Stone` 0x2E6DC, `BIGG`) | heavy | standing or crouching |
| A small stone (checkbox 1, `SMAL`) | light, small; it comes back after a shot | crouching |
| The Throw platform (Throw platform 0x27952) | heavy | standing or crouching |

The converted maps have 46 stones, 1 of them in level 1-1, and Generators spawn 3. Each is a `Spik Stone`: Lomax's decoration (object 51, whose spawn FLINK.DLL wraps) with MAIN.WLD's `Stones` records. Param 0 = small (converter `stone_type`).

**The Throw platform** (Flink's Throw platform, init 0x27952, its extra hook 0x279B8 run by `Actor_ThrownThink`). The converted maps have 8: 11-1, three in 11-2, 14-1, two in 23-1 and 24-1 (3 spawned by Generators). Each is a `Mega Throw` at Flink's placement, a static Mega (object 64, whose spawn FLINK.DLL wraps) with its `STAY` record; the Mega's x speed (with an amplitude of 0) is Flink's |param|. FLINK.DLL `throwplat.c` makes it a thrown object at rest (heavy, no fuse):
- Lomax can stand on it wherever it is, also while it flies (Flink moves it with `Platform_Stand` 0x1A29A, which carries the player; here `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer`).
- Coming down to the water surface + 5 (`g_WaterLevelY`, the tide included), it floats there, with a splash if it fell in.
- Floating, it drifts the way it faces (the way it was thrown) at |param| × 0x28F / 65536 px a tick. A wall ahead stops the drift for good: Flink's 0x1AD4C, the column at its x ± its hitbox's right, |its hitbox's top| / 16 + 1 cells up from its feet (the loop enters at its `dbra`), two for its `STAY` (top −19 / −22); FLINK.DLL's `flink_wall_ahead` (enemy.c). A wall higher up lets it drift under: in 24-1 it carries Lomax under the rock at x 1136 (its wall cells at y 208 … 223) to the Fountain at x 1368.
- Ground under its centre at or above that line beaches it: it is a thrown object at rest again.
- Landed on, it dips for 31 ticks, (15 − |t − 15|) / 2 px, with a splash. More than 5 px off its line, it moves 1 px a tick back up.
- Picked up, it stops floating.
- Floating, it is despawned only by x, as Flink's window (init 0x27952: +0xE = 8, the mid window) tests only x: never culled by Lomax (phase 3) and despawned once it is 160 px past the view's sides (512 px wide, Lomax+'s widest), its placement spawning it again. On land it has Lomax's cull phase 1. Lomax's cull tests y too (110 px past the view's bottom for phase 1, `Actors_DrawAndCull` 0x40F040, margins at 0x464540), and 11-2's tide (the Water at the map's bottom, y 304, ±72 px) takes a floating one down to y 381, 77 px under the map: with the camera high, phase 1 would despawn it every low tide. For the same reason a thrown object below the map (`below_map`: 64 px under its bottom, y 368 there) is killed only when it is also more than 64 px under the water's surface, so that 11-2's Throw platforms (two of them placed over the water at x 1260 and 1325, falling into it from their spawn) reach a low tide's surface and float instead of being killed on the way, their placements never to spawn them again.

**The button.** It is armed whenever it isn't held, and disarmed by a pick-up or a throw (`g_GrabArmed` 0x8148), so each needs a fresh press. Holding it while walking up to something picks it up.

**Picking up.** Lomax picks up the first object whose hitbox meets his grab box. He must carry nothing, not be on a rope or dying, and the input must not be locked. A heavy object can't be picked up in the air in the water. The grab box is Flink's: the second box of Flink's current animation record (0x332A0), from the feet, in front of him:
- standing (Flink's `STAY`): x 5..13, y −26..−18;
- crouching (Lomax's `DUCK`, Flink's `DUCK`): x 6..15, y −18..−9.

A chest's `OPEN` hitbox and a small stone reach only 14 px up, below the standing box, so Lomax has to crouch for them, as Flink does. A stunned dummy (27 px) or a big stone (23 px) can be taken standing.

**Carrying.** The object's own update doesn't run. FLINK.DLL places it every tick after `Player_Update`, facing Lomax's way and drawn in front of him (draw layer 2). It has no contact flags meanwhile, so Lomax neither stomps it nor is hurt by it. Where it is held:
- at Flink's hand: the attach point of Flink's pose where his carry hooks (0x1B5B2 / 0x1B5BE, through `PlayerDrawHookTable` 0x3405E) draw it (MAIN.WLD `Hero`, `HAND_HEAVY` / `HAND_LIGHT` in grab.c), from the feet, whatever Lomax's own pose:

  | | standing (`STAY`; `WALK`, `RUN` within 2 px) | crouching (`DUCK`) | in the air (`JUMP`) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | light, in front | x + 8, y − 22 | x + 10, y − 13 | x + 9, y − 24 |
  | heavy, overhead | x − 4, y − 37 | x − 2, y − 29 | x − 3, y − 39 |

- plus the object's own offset (Flink's +0x10 / +0x12): the dummy 6, 10; a small stone 4, 5.

The hand is not taken from Lomax's hitbox (heavy: 6 px under its top): his jump pose is 11 px shorter than his standing one, so a heavy object thrown in a jump would start 15 px lower than Flink's, and a big stone thrown at the top of a full jump would not reach the boss's head (its box peaking 114 px above the ground instead of 128, as Flink's numbers give: jump 37 + hand 39 + the held throw's rise 29 px).

A heavy object:
- cuts Lomax's speed to 1/4 when he lifts it;
- keeps him at 1.5 px a tick on the ground, half his walk (Flink's slow row is 1.0 of 2.0);
- lowers his jump to Flink's 37 px (Flink's boost is 0x6000 instead of 0x9000; move.c's `HEAVY_JUMP_HEIGHT`, see Movement).

While carrying, Lomax can't attack or hang on a rope, as Flink can't (its hands are full):
- no helmet throw: the Helm button's press (`g_HelmButtonMask` 0x52C6C8, read at 0x4107ED) is cleared from `g_InputPressed` before `Player_Update`;
- no air spin: neither is Jump's press in the air out of the water (it sets `g_bAirSpin` at 0x410425, and nothing else reads it there), and picking something up clears `g_bAirSpin`;
- no rope: `Rope_WaitForGrab`'s call of `Player_TryGrabHangPoint` (0x429FE0) goes through `try_hang`, which says no while he carries something. This covers the lianas and the monkeys' tails.

A light object that meets a wall or a ceiling falls out of his hands (0x1B648). A carried dummy's stun keeps running: at 0 it jumps out of his hands (4.0 up) and hurts him (0x1B66C, `Player_TakeHit`). It keeps animating while carried or thrown, as in Flink, whose actor loop steps every actor's frames whatever its think: `STUN` with its bubbles, then `WAKE` (`enemy_grab_frame` picks the frame with `stun_frame` and syncs it with `Actor_AdvanceAnim`).

**Throwing** is a fresh press while carrying:
- **Hurt** (Lomax's hurt timer goes up): he drops it, with his speed and at least 4.0 up.
- **In the water:** he drops it (`Player_DropCarried` 0x1A778), with his speed and at least 2.0 up.
- **Heavy:** vx = his vx / 4 ± 1.5, vy = his vy / 16 + 1.5 up, plus a boost of 0x7800 (0x5000 when he stands on a seesaw: Flink 0x330A4, `g_PlatformActor` 0x82FE with the seesaw's think; `seesaw_under_lomax`). While Ability stays held, the boost shrinks by 0x800 a tick and is added to vy, and half of it to vx: held, the big stone flew 98 px instead of 45. Sound: Lomax's THROW2. The start speeds, the boost (applied before the move, its first tick 0x7000), 0x1B0E2's gravity and friction, and the wall, ceiling and landing tests are Flink's (0x33066, `Actor_ThrownThink` 0x323C2), in the same order. The vx / 4 comes from Lomax's own speed (`MOVE_SPEED`): up to 2.4 in the air where Flink's is 2.0, so a running jump throw goes 0.1 px a tick further.
- **On a platform:** a heavy thrown object lands on a "dont move" Mega platform and makes it sink (see "Platforms"); it lands on no other platform, as in Flink, where only that platform calls 0x1A1B2 (and the Fountain, not converted).
- **Light, standing still with Up held:** straight up at his vy / 16 + 8.0 (about 130 px for the dummy). THROW2.
- **Light:** a shot (below). THROW1.

**A thrown object** (`Actor_ThrownThink`):
- falls at 0.25 px a tick (at most 4.0); its vx is braked by 0x800 a tick in the air, 0x6000 on the ground;
- bounces off walls, turning (Lomax's pot-bounce sound for Flink's 0x29), and off ceilings (2.0 down);
- in the water it falls at 1.0 at most, with a splash as it goes in (`Water_SpawnSplash`);
- can be picked up again, also in the air;
- landing, it plays Lomax's own landing sound (FALL1, 0x0C, `Player_Update` 0x410685), unless it was lying at rest; at the full 4.0 it bounces once (2.0 up, vx halved);
- once landed, a dummy gets its own update back (still stunned, or it hops and walks on); a chest or a stone stays a thrown object at rest, and slides down 45° slopes at 2.0;
- while it moves, the first Flink enemy it touches dies: Flink sets that actor's hit flag, which kills every enemy kind at once (`enemy_struck`). Lomax's poof shows at the object's front for Flink's puff (0x3365A). A thrown dummy then dies too; a chest or a stone flies on;
- it never hurts Lomax: in Flink, once its lifetime is over (14 ticks after being dropped, 30 after a heavy throw, 1000 after a throw straight up) it hurts Flink if it falls on him; here not, nor when it comes back off a wall or the boss's trunks.

**A shot** (0x32630). The object becomes Flink's spinning Shot: MAIN.WLD's `SPIN`, or `SMAL` for a small object, stored as `SHSM` (converter `shot_records`, in the dummy's and the stones' types).
- It flies straight on at 4.0 px a tick, starting at 1.5 up and falling at 0.1875 a tick (at most 4.0).
- It bounces off walls and ceilings, and off the ground at half its speed when it lands faster than 1.0; slower, it rolls on.
- Every Flink enemy it touches dies (a poof each), and it flies on.
- It never hurts Lomax, even coming back off a wall (Flink's hurts him after 14 frames).
- It blinks from 420 ticks and goes at 540, or when Lomax's camera despawns it off screen.
- Its placement is forgotten for good (`forget_placement`), except a small stone's, which comes back.
- A dummy thrown as a shot is out of the game: it counts as defeated then (the HUD's count, a spirit, and a coin at odd x).

**How it is hooked.** `Game_Frame` calls `Player_Update` at 0x404C74, and that call goes through `player_tick`:
- before `Player_Update` it handles the button, the pick-up and the throw (Flink's `Player_HandleButtons` runs before the actors think);
- after it, it places the carried object (Flink's carry hooks draw it at the hand).

A carried, thrown or shot object runs FLINK.DLL's update; its own update, contact flags, draw layer and cull are kept and given back when it lands. All of this happens only in Flink's maps.

## Trees (Flink's Tree actor)

Flink's `Tree` actor (0x30B22) is not a tree but the column of vine sprites that passes in front of the screen in FOREST and VILLAGE maps (11 converted maps have one). It is always spawned, and its draw hook (0x30BC2) puts its `TREE` sprite 8 times down the screen, 32 px apart:
- x on screen = `((0x25F − P) & 0x1FF) − 0x80`, P = (`g_CameraX` × num / den + param 0) & 0x1FF: one column every 512 px of a scroll faster than the camera;
- y = −(`g_CameraY` × num / den & 31) + 32 k;
- num / den = 100 / 70, with the sprite's priority bit set: in front of everything. With checkbox 1 (FOREST 15 and 16, MOUNTAIN 5 and 8, all Priority maps) it is 70 / 100 without the priority bit: behind the play map's tiles, over the background.

The converter makes it a `Spik Tree` decoration (object 51) on plane 0, or with checkbox 1 on the background plane. Its record is the sprite 10 times down (Lomax's view is 240 px high, Flink's 224, and y wraps every 64 px, see below) and again 512 px to the left, for widescreen views. FLINK.DLL gives it a draw function (`tree_draw`) that places it on the screen (actor flag 0x40) by Flink's formula from the play plane's camera, after the cameras are set for the frame, except that y is −(camera y × num / den & 63): the same picture, since the sprite repeats every 32 px, but a wrap is a 63 px jump. Lomax+'s FPS Override draws frames between ticks with every sprite moved part of the way, and interpolates moves of up to 48 px a tick; Flink's 31 px wrap would be interpolated as a slide the wrong way, and the Trees would flicker whenever the camera moved up or down at more than 60 fps. A jump over 48 px is drawn as is. Lomax spawns objects only as they scroll into view, so the converter repeats the placement every 320 × 240 px across the plane; FLINK.DLL keeps the first one that spawns and never despawns it.

## Knols

Flink's `Knol spiky` (0x2B1FC, think 0x2B214) is a spiked ball on a thorny vine (the vine is map tiles). It loops its `KNOL` animation (2 frames) and hurts Flink wherever his hitbox meets its own (0x1A462), from above too: it can't be stomped. The converted maps have 7, in FOREST: five in 2-1, two in 4-2.

Each becomes a `Spik Knol`, Lomax's decoration (object 51) with Flink's `KNOL` record and hitbox (−8, −8, 8, 8). It needs no FLINK.DLL code: `Deco_Spawn` (0x427060) sets the actor's flags 0x200020 (it hurts, whatever its animation is called) and `Deco_Update` (0x427090) only advances the animation. Without the hittable flag 0x10000, `Player_Update`'s contact loop calls `Player_TakeHit` on any overlap, falling onto it included, instead of a stomp.

## Swinging balls

Flink's `Ball (swinging)` with checkbox 8 "deadly" (the same code as its Swingplatform: init 0x2B3EA, the pivot's think 0x2B5E8, each ball's think 0x2B6BA and draw 0x2B752) is a pivot with `arms` spiked balls turning round it, or, with 0 arms, one ball swinging as a pendulum. The converted maps have 45: FOREST 3-5 (seven 3- and 4-arm wheels and a pendulum), 5-2, 6-1, 6-2; VILLAGE 10-3, 11-1, 11-2, 13-1, 14-1, 14-2, 15-2, 16-1; MOUNTAIN 18-2, 24-1.

- Params: `length` (the balls are (length − 1) × 16 px out), `speed`, `arms`, `winkel`, `pre-run`; checkboxes `dont move` (1), `small` (2), `knob` (4: the pivot draws `KNOB`), `deadly` (8). Without `knob` the pivot draws its `RING` record (init 0x2B472 sets `RING` on it, `KNOB` only with the checkbox; its draw 0x2B6B2 is `Actor_DrawFrame`): a link at the pivot itself, before the chain's from 16 px out.
- Angles are 2048 a turn (byte offsets into a 1024-entry sine table at 0x1491E, the same scale as Lomax's), 0 straight down; ball k is at the pivot's angle + k × 2048 / arms, at pivot + r × (sin a, cos a).
- A wheel turns at `speed` a frame. With `dont move` it turns only while ridden, which a deadly one never is, so it stands still.
- A pendulum starts at the bottom at |speed|; its speed changes by −¼ a frame, the change reversing once |speed| is reached, so it swings to either side. Then, if `winkel` is set, an angle within |speed| of `winkel` × 2 reverses the speed (a bounce, sound 0x18; VILLAGE's paired pendulums in 14-2 at 8 and 1008).
- The pivot's think runs `pre-run` times at the start.
- **Draw order:** the init picks its draw list from its spawn flags' bits 2–3 (table 0x2B40C: 0 → list 3, 4 → list 2, 8 → list 1, 0xC → list 0), and its balls take the pivot's. `Actors_DrawAll` (0x33356) draws list 0, list 1, then Flink, then lists 2 and 3, the first drawn in front. Most balls are on list 3, behind Flink with the platforms (the Wall platforms are on list 3 too); 13-1's and 16-1's (0x04) are on list 2, behind Flink but in front of list 3, so 13-1's chain swings over the Wall platforms; 5-2's, 6-2's and 11-2's (0x08) are on list 1, in front of Flink. FLINK.DLL gives the ball Lomax's draw layer for its list (`FLINK_LAYER` in flink.h: lists 0, 1, Flink, 2, 3 are Lomax's layers 3, 2, Lomax's 6, 1, 0); the converter passes the list as the `Spik Ball`'s 7th param.
- Each ball hurts Flink when its hitbox (`BIGG`'s) meets his (0x1A462). Each draws its chain, a `RING` link every 16 px from the pivot out to the ball, drawn as the lianas' (the sprite's top-left 8 px up-left of the point).

Each becomes a `Spik Ball`, a Lomax decoration (object 51; converter: the balls block in `convert`) with records `MAIN` (the knob, or without it the pivot's `RING`), `BALL` and `RING`, and params radius, arms, speed, bounce angle (`winkel` × 2, −1 for none), pre-run and `dont move`. FLINK.DLL's `dll\balls.c` (`ball_spawn` from `spik_spawn`) gives the one actor at the pivot Flink's motion (`ball_turn`), the balls' hurt test (`Player_TakeHit`; the pivot touches nothing) and the drawing: one `Actor_Draw` per sprite with the actor moved onto it, so that Lomax+'s FPS Override, which follows each `Actor_Draw` call, moves each piece smoothly; balls, then the knob, then the links (the first queued is in front). Lomax's own cull, by the pivot, would make a ball still on screen vanish, so the actor is never culled by the game and despawns itself once the pivot is 96 px plus its radius beyond a 512 × 240 view; its placement spawns it again, restarting it, as Flink's restarts outside its window. A Generator's ball (flag 0x2000) is tested in x only, as Flink's watcher (0x2DFB0) tests its group: despawned for its y it would not come back by proximity, which cost 6-2's second row (y 398 … 511, its Generator at (318, 286)) its lower balls. The bounce sound isn't played.

## Stahlkugeln

Flink's `Stahlkugel` (Stahlkugel; init 0x24A24, VILLAGE) is a spiked ball on a chain that swings toward and away from the screen, loops round that way, or turns round a vertical axis with its chain as a cone, drawn at 7 sizes for its depth. The converted maps have 21: 13-2 (two swinging, the three turning on one pivot at (744, 83), a row of five spawned by a Generator, as a wave), 14-2 (two turning) and 15-1 (one deadly turning spoke, three deadly swings, five deadly ones that loop).

- Params: `length`, `speed`, `x-offset`, `y-offset`, `pre-run`; checkboxes `rotating` (1), `deadly` (2), `turn around` (4). The first placement of each map (spawn flag bit 6, no params) only builds the scaled sprites (0x24D88: `EINS`, `ZWEI`, `DREI` each at 70, 80 … 130 %) and is left out.
- Angles are 2048 a turn (the sine table at 0x1491E, Lomax's scale), 0 straight down; the pivot is the placement − (8, 9); the think runs `pre-run` times at the start.
- **Swinging** (think 0x24B32, draw 0x24B60): the speed starts at `speed` and changes by −¼ a frame, the change reversing once |speed| reaches `speed`, as the swinging balls' pendulum; with `turn around` (think 0x24B22) the angle goes on by `speed` a frame, a full loop. Link k (k = 0 … length, the ball last) is d = 14k px from the pivot: y + d cos a on the screen, z = d sin a toward the viewer.
- **Rotating** (think 0x24B22, draw 0x24C08): the angle goes on by `speed` a frame; link k is d = k × `x-offset` px out, at x + d sin(a + 1024) and depth d cos(a + 1024), and k × `y-offset` px down.
- **Size:** z / D + 3, clamped to 0…6 (70 % + 10 % a step), D = (14 × length − 14) / 3 swinging, length × x-offset / 3 − 1 rotating.
- **In front or behind:** with z ≥ 0 (angle 0…1024 swinging, 512…1536 rotating) it is in draw layer 1, which Flink's `Actors_DrawAll` (0x33356) draws before Flink, so in front of him, from the ball in; otherwise in layer 2, behind him, from the pivot out. It is a low-priority sprite: behind the priority tiles.
- **Hurting** (0x24CD2, 0x1A46E): only in front and at sizes 2…4 (unclamped), so a ball swinging away from the screen can be walked under. The ball hurts in a 24 × 24 box round its middle; with `deadly` each link (`EINS`, spiked) in a 16 × 16 one; without, the links (`DREI`) never hurt.
- **Placement of the sprites:** the scaled sprites are centred where the full-size one is (0x1DE66: (w − scaled w) / 2), and the full-size ones are drawn with their top-left at the point, the ball 8 px left and 6 up: the links' middle is the point + (8, 8), the ball's + (8, 10), the placement + (0, −1) and + (0, 1).

Each becomes a `Spik Stahl`, a Lomax decoration (object 51; converter: the stahls block in `convert`) with records `MAIN` (blank), `BALL` (`ZWEI`), `LINK` (`EINS`) and `RING` (`DREI`), each one sprite anchored at its middle, and params Flink's five plus the checkboxes. FLINK.DLL's `dll\stahlkugel.c` (`stahl_spawn` from `spik_spawn`) gives the one actor at the placement Flink's motion (`stahl_turn`), the hurt test (`Player_TakeHit`; the actor itself touches nothing) and the drawing: one `Actor_Draw` per sprite with the actor moved onto it, at Flink's size through `Actor_Draw`'s scale (per mille, round the anchor, as Lomax shrinking into a door), in Flink's order (the first queued is in front), in draw layer 2 (in front of Lomax) or 1 (behind him). As the swinging balls, it is never culled by the game and despawns itself 96 px plus its reach beyond a 512 × 240 view; its placement or Generator spawns it again.

## Spikes

Flink's `Spike` (init 0x2856E, think 0x28608; VILLAGE and TREE) is a spike that shoots out of the ground on a timer. The converted maps have 32, all pointing up and most in rows that a Generator spawns: 10-4 (6), 13-1 (9: two pairs, each spawned by a Generator, and a row of five, a wave), 14-2 (13, a wave) and 25-1 (4).

- Params: `hidden` frames, `out` frames (Flink adds 30) and `pre-run` frames; checkbox 1 `horizontal` (the `HORI` record, moving its x) and 2 the other way; neither is set in the converted maps, so they are left out.
- It starts hidden, 32 px under its placement, where the ground hides it. The countdown starts at `hidden`, then the step runs `pre-run` times: a row's pre-runs (40, 30 … 0 in 13-1's five) make it a wave.
- **Each frame** (0x28608): fully out, touching Flink hurts him (0x1A462, his hitbox against its `VERT` hitbox); then the step (0x2861C). When the countdown runs out the phase flips: hiding for `hidden` frames, or coming out for `out` + 30.
- **Coming out:** up 4 px a frame to 24 px under the placement (its tip just showing), a 30-frame wait there, sound 0x36 (when on screen: here its placement's point within the view, since the actor is 24 px lower then, off the screen where the floor is at its bottom edge, as in 25-1; here Lomax's pop-up spike's sound 0x5C, which `Spit_PopUpSpikes_Update` 0x41EA70 plays as it shoots up), then up 4 px a frame to the placement, where it stays the rest of the phase. **Hiding:** down 4 px a frame to 32 px under it.

Each becomes a `Spik Spike`, a Lomax decoration (object 51; converter: the spikes block in `convert`) with Flink's `VERT` record and hitbox and params hidden, out, pre-run. FLINK.DLL's `dll\spike.c` (`spike_spawn` from `spik_spawn`) moves the actor to the spike's y and clips it at its placement (+0x7C, as the chests: the ground hides what is below), and does Flink's hurt test (`Player_TakeHit`; the actor itself touches nothing, so it can't be stomped). Generators spawn and despawn them as their other objects (see Hidden objects).

## Drop balls

Flink's `Drop Ball` (init 0x27B24, think 0x27B8E, chain draw 0x27C60) is a spiked ball on a chain that drops onto the floor on a timer and is pulled back up. Only 10-3 (VILLAGE map 6) has them: 8, in two rows of four (x 230-440 on the lower floor, 1040-1235 on the upper one, the fourth of each row, at 440 and 1167, spawned by a Generator), all with `fall-height` 80 and `fall-timer` 50 or 10, alternating.

- **Init:** home is the placement's y, the floor home + `fall-height`; the countdown and its reload are `fall-timer`. Then the step runs `$10(a4)` times: the word after the placement's two params, which is the next GNMY entry's size (14 to 22 here). The converter passes it as the third param.
- **Each frame:** touching the ball hurts Flink (0x1A462, his hitbox against `BALL`'s, the ball only); then the step, by its 16.16 speed: **waiting** at home until the countdown runs out (speed 0.25); **falling**, speed + 0.25 a frame up to 4.0, until the floor: sound 0x35 when on screen (here Lomax's 0x58, as the boss's landing, which is Flink's 0x35 too), 30 frames there; **lying**, shaking ±1 px (0x27BEC: 1, 1, −1, −1 by the countdown) while the countdown is 14 or more; **pulled up** at 1 px a frame to home, where the countdown (reloaded) starts again.
- **Draw:** the actor's `BALL` (the ball and the chain's lowest link), and its draw hook the `APPE` link every 32 px up from the ball until one is off the top of the screen. The hook runs at `BALL`'s hook point (x − 3, y − 33, Actor_DrawFrame 0x32972), where `BALL`'s own link starts, and Sprite_DrawScreen puts each link's top-left 32 px further up from it, so the chain is one straight line. The chain is on draw list 3, behind Flink.

Each becomes a `Spik Drop`, a Lomax decoration (object 51; converter: the drops block in `convert`) with Flink's `BALL` and `APPE` records (the link at the hook's offsets) and params fall-height, fall-timer, pre-run. FLINK.DLL's `dll\drop.c` (`drop_spawn` from `spik_spawn`) runs the step, does the hurt test (`Player_TakeHit`; the actor itself touches nothing, so it can't be stomped) and draws the ball, then the links, one `Actor_Draw` each (for Lomax+'s FPS Override, as the swinging balls), on Lomax's layer 0 (`FLINK_LAYER(3)`). Lomax culls and respawns it by its point as any decoration.

## Falling apples

Flink's `Falling apple` (init 0x28E02, think 0x28E30; param `distance`) is an apple hanging in a tree that shakes when Flink walks under it, then falls and bursts. The converted maps have 21: 8 in 25-1 (TREE map 3, `distance` 48), 12 in 25-2 (map 8, 24: a row of six under a ledge, spawned by a Generator, and a row of five over the floor) and one in 28-1 (map 7, 0: only straight under it). 11 of them are spawned by Generators.

- **Init:** draw list 3 (behind Flink), +6 bit 3 (thrown objects and Flink's slide reach it), `HANG`.
- **Each frame,** in this order: hit (+3 bit 6: a thrown object, a spell, the slide) → the burst; touching Flink (0x1A462: its hitbox against his) → the burst, after his slide knocks it away or, not sliding, `Player_TakeHit`; while Flink's hurt timer runs it touches nothing. Then its animation advances (`HANG` and `WARN` loop), and:
  - **hanging:** Flink 40 px or more below it and `distance` px or less across → 30 frames of `WARN` (it shakes);
  - **shaking:** at the end, `HANG` again and it falls;
  - **falling:** not on the screen → freed (Actor_Free 0x32D9E: not forgotten, back after a lost life); otherwise its speed + 0.25 a frame, with no limit, and once Map_GetGroundHeight (0x1AEEA, 101 cells down) finds ground at or above it → the burst.
- **The burst** (0x28EF8): `DEAD`, sound 0x48 when on screen, and Actor_ForgetForever (0x1B1E8): gone for good, `DEAD`'s frame (five pieces of apple) broken up into its sprites, flying up and falling.

Each becomes a `Spik Apple`, a Lomax decoration (object 51; converter: the apples block in `convert`) with Flink's `HANG` and `WARN` (and their hitbox) and `DEAD` as `BOOM`; param `distance`. FLINK.DLL's `srcpple.c` (`apple_spawn` from `spik_spawn`) runs the think. The actor touches nothing itself, so it can't be stomped: landing on it hurts, as in Flink. Lomax's `TUMB` (his roll) takes the slide's place and bursts it without hurting him; his spin is no slide, so an apple hurts him while he spins. Lomax's thrown helmet doesn't reach it. Thrown objects burst it through grab.c's `strike` (`apple_struck`). The burst is `forget_placement` (gone for the level visit, `placement_gone` in its spawn) and `Actor_ShatterIntoPieces` with `BOOM`, with Lomax's enemy hit (SND_HIT) for 0x48, as the Buzzard's. "On the screen" is `enemy_on_screen` (Lomax's view, 32 px margin), and freed is `Actor_Kill`. The ground is searched from the y before each move, so a fast fall can't step past a floor.

## Apple (big) and App2

Flink's `Apple (big)` (init 0x24242) and `App2` (init 0x24204) are TREE's rolling apples. Both run the think 0x242CE and the draw 0x245FE; +0x4C tells them apart. Params: `start speed` (1/100 px a frame, × 0x28F) and checkbox 1 `16 phases`. The converted maps have 9 Apple (big) (26-1 two, 27-2 one, 28-1 six) and 17 App2 (27-2 six, 28-1 eleven); all but 27-2's Apple (big) at (314, 112) are spawned by Generators. The first placement of each type in a map (spawn flag bit 6, 0x24724) only builds the rotated frames and is freed; the converter leaves it out, and its `16 phases` gives the others theirs (16 everywhere).

- **Init:** draw list 1, vx = `start speed` × 0x28F, `ROLL`. App2 also gets +6 bits 2 (no fuse), 3 (thrown objects and the slide reach it) and 6 (its hook 0x242B2 while thrown), and its carry offset (5, 8).
- **Each frame,** in this order:
  - **hit** (+3 bit 6, App2 only: a thrown object, Flink's slide, a rolling apple) → `DEAD`, sound 0x48 when on screen, broken up (0x1B1F8, not forgotten: back after a lost life).
  - **the move** (0x1B0E2): vy − 0.125 (at most 4.0 down), then y and x.
  - **landing** (not rising; Actor_SnapToGround 0x1AE96: the ground from y − 16, 4 cells, within the table 0x1AEDA's reach for vx's whole px): on it, and landing faster than 2.0 bounces: the Apple (big) at 4.0 up, with the screen shaking 0x13 frames (0x874E) and sound 0x18 when on screen and the screen isn't shaking already; App2 at 3/4 of its speed, sound 0x29. Slower, it stops falling. A bounce at 4.0 rises 62 px and lands again at 3.875, so an Apple (big) that once lands faster than 2.0 keeps bouncing until it lands higher up. Then on flat ground it slows by 0x400 a frame above 1.0 and stops under 0.625, at phase 0 only, upright; on a slope it speeds up downhill by 0x800 a frame (0x8000 the frame it bounces), twice while still going uphill, up to 1.5.
  - **the phase** + vx (16 phases a turn, one every 4 px).
  - **stomped** (Actor_CheckStompedByPlayer 0x1A352): Flink bounces off it, sound 0x18; nothing else that frame.
  - **otherwise:** in the air the touch test (0x1A462: Player_TakeHit; App2 is knocked away at 2.0 and hit by Flink's slide). On the ground, App2 at rest can be picked up (Actor_CheckPickedUp 0x1ABB4) and rolling it touches. The Apple (big), rolling and not pushed last frame, touches while within 20 px of Flink across or rolling toward him; otherwise the push check (Actor_CheckPushedByPlayer 0x1AA56): pushed by Flink on the ground faster than 0.625, it rolls at his speed (at most 1.0) unless it already rolls faster or the other way. On flat ground it keeps rolling at 0.625–1.0 after he stops.
  - **facing** from vx; moving, the touch test on other actors (Actor_FindTouchingActor 0x1A5A0: every other slot by the frame's parity, the first with +6 bit 3 it touches is hit: the enemies die, App2s break, Falling apples burst); a wall at its front (0x1AD4C) turns it back.
- **App2 thrown** (hook 0x242B2): once its vy is 0 it gets its think back and rolls on at its speed. Thrown straight up, that is at the top of the throw (vy 8.0 less 0.25 a frame reaches 0 exactly), and it falls from there under its own think, hurting Flink if it lands on him. Thrown while walking it is a shot (grab.c), forgotten.
- **The draw** (0x245FE): phase 0 is `ROLL`; phases 1–7 the frames rotated at 0x1DC56 in 22.5° steps (0x40 of 0x400); 8–15 the same drawn upside down (sprite flags 0x1800). Flink's facing mirrors the hitbox only.
- **Spawned after the level's start** (0x879E set), its first draw is 0x245C0: drawn on the screen, it is hidden and freed 30 frames later. Not ported (see Hidden objects).

Each becomes a `Spik Appl` / `Spik App2`, a Lomax decoration (object 51; converter: the rolling apples block in `convert`, `bigapple_records`, `rolled_frames`) whose `ROLL` has the 16 frames: `ROLL`'s sprite rotated clockwise about its frame's middle (nearest pixel, the same size), then those turned upside down, each at Flink's draw offsets (`ROLL_DRAW`). App2 also has `DEAD` and MAIN.WLD's Shot `SPIN`. Params: start speed, phases. FLINK.DLL's `bigapple.c` (`bigapple_spawn` from `spik_spawn`) runs the think and holds the phase's frame. The actor touches nothing in Lomax's contact loop: the stomp and the touch are Flink's tests against Lomax's hitbox, the stomp Lomax's enemy bounce (with stomp.c's high bounce on Jump), the touch `Player_TakeHit`; Lomax's roll (`TUMB`) is the slide. The push is rock.c's (`push_check`, `rock_after`): Lomax's `PUSH` pose at 1.0 px a tick. App2 is picked up and thrown through grab.c (`bigapple_grab_kind`, hook `app2_after`); thrown objects break it (`bigapple_struck` in grab.c's `strike`). The touch test on other actors reaches the Flink enemies (`enemy_struck`), the Falling apples, the Raketen wurms and App2s; not the Rocks (none in TREE). The shake is razorback.c's (Flink's counter, `flink_quake`). Sounds: ROCKHITX for 0x18, the pot bounce for 0x29, the enemy hit for 0x48; the break-up plays no 0x2A (+3 bit 5).

Rolling App2s break each other: in 27-2 the three from the Generator at x 551 (the last one at 2.0, the others 1.5) meet at the slope's foot, and in 28-1 the Apple (big) rolling down breaks the App2s ahead of it.

## Rolling stones

Flink's `Rolling stone` (init 0x23E26, think 0x23E74, draw 0x24062; MOUNTAIN map 14 = 23-1) is a stone ball standing in the shallow lava, which the player crosses on it. The converted map has 5 (x 1238, 1395, 1590, 1878, 2001); the map's first placement (x 191, spawn flag bit 6) only builds the rotated frames and is left out, its checkbox 1 giving the others 16 phases. Each is a 'Spik Rolling' (Lomax's decoration, object 51) with `ROLL` at 16 angles, as the Apple (big)'s (`bigapple_records`, the draw offsets of 0x24062), run by FLINK.DLL `rollingstone.c`:
- **Platform.** It is a platform (Flink's `Platform_Stand` 0x1A29A; here `Platform_MoveAndCarryPlayer`, which also moves it by its vx and carries Lomax), its top rounded: the hitbox's top lowered by {0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 2, 3, 4, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8}[|Lomax's x − its x| / 2] (0x2400A).
- **Rolling.** Flink's: standing on it, its vx is minus the player's, so walking on it rolls it, and him with it, the other way, as a ball rolls under feet; with Left or Right held his x also moves by that vx (his own walk is taken back), and every 8th frame he is moved 1 px toward its middle; landing divides his speed by 4; left alone it slows by 1/32 a frame. Lomax's walk is faster than Flink's and slides on after a release, which would roll the stone at his full speed and walk him off it when he stops, so the ride here is Lomax's barrel's (Barrel_Update 0x426D20) on flat ground: until he first walks on it his walk is held to 2.0 a tick, scaled as his speed (`move_speed`: 1.6); walking on it rolls it as Flink's, moving him back by its vx and 1 px toward its middle every 8th tick; with no Left or Right held (or while it still rolls its own way under a new rider) he goes 2/3 of its vx with it, and it slows by 1/64 a tick; Jump pressed sets his vx to 0. Jumping and walking off work as on any platform.
- **Blocked.** A wall ahead (0x1AD4C: the cells at its hitbox's side, |its top| / 16 + 1 up from its feet; `flink_wall_ahead`) or another Rolling stone within x ± 28, y − 48 … y (0x1A65A) blocks it: standing on it without the pad held that way stops it; pressing that way still rolls it back.
- **Drawn.** Its phase moves by its vx; the frame is the phase's whole px / 4 & 15 (8 phases: / 2). Draw list 3 (`FLINK_LAYER(3)`).
- Flink sets 0x828F (Player_Update's slide timer) to 60 while it is stood on; Lomax has no such timer. The speed limit is Lomax's `g_PlayerSpeedCapOverride` (0x49C4B4), cleared when he leaves it. A stone out of the view goes back to its placement (Flink's window 0; here Lomax's culling).

## Butterflies

Flink's `Butterflies` (type 38, init 0x247D8, think 0x2488A, each pair's draw 0x2497C; params `how many` (pairs), `x-speed`, `y-speed`, checkbox 1 "Move left") are decoration: pairs of butterflies flying up from a point. They touch nothing. The converted maps have 8: one in 25-1 (TREE map 3, 8 pairs, y-speed 100), three in 26-1 (map 4, 16 pairs each, "Move left"), three in 27-1 (map 5, 8–10 pairs, y-speed 50) and one in 25-2 (map 8, "Move left"). 25-1's and 26-1's are spawned by Generators (height 200).

- **Init** (the spawner): hidden, activation window 0x18 (never culled), y + 20; without "Move left" the x-speed is negated and the sprites are flipped. Its sprites get the priority bit (+3 bit 7): in front of the high-priority tiles. It takes the `FLY ` record's five frames' hardware sprites (0x34132).
- **Each frame:** every 20 frames, starting at once, it makes a child with two butterflies; after `how many` children it is freed (Actor_Free 0x32D9E: not forgotten). Per butterfly, in random order (0x19C8A): x = the spawner's + (rand & 127), y = the spawner's + (rand & 31), x speed = (x-speed + (rand & 31), the random part with x-speed's sign) × −655 and y speed = (y-speed + (rand & 31)) × −655 (16.16: 1/100 px a frame), so they fly up, left with "Move left"; then each one's flap speed, (4 + (rand & 3)) / 16 of a frame a frame.
- **The child** (draw list 1: in front of Flink) has an empty think; its draw moves both butterflies and draws each one's frame (counter & 7) of the table 0x24A14 {0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 3, 2, 1} with `Sprite_DrawScreen` (0x3430E: the hardware sprite's top-left at the point, only inside −32 … 320 × −32 … 224 of the screen). While neither is on the screen its lifetime (200 at first, 60 again each time one is) runs down, and at its end the child is freed.

Each becomes a `Spik Butterfly`, a Lomax decoration (object 51; converter: the butterflies block in `convert`) with records `FLY ` (the five sprites, each a one-piece frame at the point less the sprite's offset in its ANIM frame) and `FLYR` (the same flipped in place); params how many, x-speed, y-speed, "Move left". FLINK.DLL's `dll\butterfly.c` (`butterfly_spawn` from `spik_spawn`) runs it: the spawner is the placement's actor, drawn as nothing, never culled, `Actor_Kill`ed when done (back after a lost life, as Flink's); a Generator's group may despawn it earlier, as Flink's watcher does. The pairs are `Actor_Alloc` children, never culled, moved in their update and drawn by two `Actor_Draw` calls a tick, the actor moved onto each butterfly (Lomax+'s FPS Override follows each call's anchor). They are drawn through plane 0 (Flink's priority tiles, a ScrollMap with the play plane's camera) on `FLINK_LAYER(1)`, so in front of the priority tiles and of Lomax. The on-screen test is Flink's box over Lomax's view (as `enemy_on_screen`); at the lifetime's end the child is `Actor_Despawn`ed.

## Rope slides

Flink's `Rope slide` (init 0x27EB8, waiting 0x27F0C, ridden 0x27F8C) is a handle on a cable; the cable is background art.
