**Movement.** `dll\move.c`, around Player_Update (`player_tick`, grab.c), from the defines at its top (`MOVE_SPEED` 80, `JUMP_HEIGHT` 68, `HEAVY_JUMP_HEIGHT` 37, `WATER_SPEED` 50, `WATER_GRAVITY` 30, `WATER_JUMP` 50, `SWIM_GAP` 20); Flink's levels only (not the title or world map):
- *Speed.* `g_PlayerMoveTable` (0x464450, data): 10 rows of {left limit, rest speed, right limit, acceleration, brake over the limit, friction}, 16.16 px a tick, the row by ground type + 1 (0 in the air, 1 flat, 2 gentle slope, 3 steep slope) + 4 with Run; 8 pushing, 9 dying. `move_set` (from `world_load`) writes the limits and rest speeds of rows 0-2 and 4-6 at `MOVE_SPEED` (80%: walking 2.4, running 3.2, in the air 2.4; Lomax's 3.0, 4.0, 3.0) in Flink's maps and Lomax's table back for his levels. The steep slopes' forced slides (rows 3 and 7) and rows 8-9 keep Lomax's speeds on land. Under water rows 0-7 go to `WATER_SPEED` of that. The caps carrying something heavy (grab.c) and on mud (groundtype.c) scale the same way (`move_speed`).
- *Jump.* Lomax's: vy -4.0 and a hold force `g_JumpHoldForce` of 0x9800, after a takeoff of 10 ticks standing or 5 moving; then each tick with Jump held the force drops by 0x800 and is taken off vy, and gravity adds 0x6000: 81 px, a tap 19. Flink's (0x1CE2E, 0x1C160): vy 4.0 and a boost of 0x9000 dropping by 0x800 and added from the next frame, gravity 0x6000: 68 px, a tap 19; carrying something heavy, 0x6000: 37 px. Since Flink's boost starts a frame later, the start force for a height comes from simulating Lomax's jump (`hold_for`: 0x8AC0 for 68 px, 0x5B40 for 37). It is set on the first tick after the takeoff, while the force is still 0x9800, so the air spin (Jump pressed again with the force under 0x9000) starts no earlier than in Lomax's levels. Its last step is held at 0x800 so that the force ends at 0: below 0 with Jump still held, landing would jump again. The bounce pad's and the water's jump-out forces are left alone.
- *Under water* (`SWIM_MODE` 2): see Water. Gravity: before Player_Update adds its 0x6000, vy is lowered so the sum is `WATER_GRAVITY`'s share, and kept under that share of 6.0. Strokes and the jump out: see Water.
- *Stomp bounce* (`dll\stomp.c`). Flink: a stomp (`Actor_CheckStompedByPlayer` 0x1A352, called by enemies, chests and the boss) sets `g_PlayerBounceReq` (0x8290), and the next frame (0x1B94E) bounces Flink with VelY 5.0, or, with Jump held in `g_Joypad` (0x870A) on that frame, a full jump: `Player_StartJump` (0x1CE2E, no jump sound) gives VelY 4.0 and the boost 0x9000 (0x6000 carrying something heavy), which lifts him while Jump stays held. Here: after `Player_BounceOffEnemy` (vy −4.0, force 0; `contact_bounce`), with Jump held `stomp_bounce` sets `g_JumpHoldForce` to a full jump's start force (`move_jump_force`: 0x8AC0, heavy 0x5B40, `WATER_JUMP`'s share under water), so the bounce rises 68 px (37 heavy), and letting go cuts it short as a tap. Without Jump the bounce is Flink's 5.0 (`BOUNCE_VY`, Lomax's is 4.0), except under water, where it stays Lomax's. Lomax's air spin: a Jump press in the air (force under 0x9000, Player_Update 0x410726) starts the `SPIN` attack, which would hit the enemy instead of stomping it (the Firemonster and Thief die of it, with no bounce). So a press that would land Lomax on a stompable actor (hurts + hittable, his plane) within `STOMP_WINDOW` (10) ticks, by his fall simulated with Lomax's gravity (`stomp_ahead`), is taken out of `g_InputPressed`: no spin, and the bounce, Jump being held, is the high one. If no bounce comes by `STOMP_GRACE` (3) ticks after the predicted landing (the contact loop bounces him a tick or two after his feet reach the head), the press is given back and he spins then. An earlier press is Lomax's spin. Not under water (strokes take Jump there).
- *Stomping several enemies at once* (`dll\contact.c`; landing on 13-2's clumps of Little Dudes hurts Lomax in his own loop, never Flink). Lomax's contact loop (0x40FEDD) takes the actors he touches one by one in slot order and bounces him off one only if he is in the air, falling (vy > 0) and last tick's feet (his contact box, [esp+0x2C], not his animation's hitbox) were above its top; any other touch hurts. Its first bounce sets vy −4.0 and lifts him, so every other enemy he touches that tick hurts him. In Flink each enemy's think asks `Actor_CheckStompedByPlayer` (0x1A352) on its own against the same player (in the air, out of the water, VelY under 2.0 up, not hurt or dying, his feet from 2 px above its top down to half its height, at most 20 px), and the stomp only requests the bounce for the next frame, so every enemy under him is stomped. Here `contact_hit` first gives a stompable Flink enemy (`enemy_stompable`: hittable, not dead, not the boss or a Raketen wurm, whose stomps are their own; a stunned one too: stomped again it dies) Flink's test, with Lomax's y and vy from before this tick's bounce (`contact_bounce` keeps them); passed, it is stomped (`enemy_bounce`), and Lomax bounces off it only if he hasn't this tick. The stub passes the loop's box pointer for his feet.

**Objects.** The spawn functions of objects 0 (the Start trapdoor, see Arrivals), 8 (`Forc`, see Scrolling screens), 37 (the Music trigger, see Music), 3 (`Revi`), 51 (`Spik`), 64 (`Mega`), 67 (`Rope`), 65 (`Trir`), 74 (`Muta`), 85 (`Pots`) and 86 (`Gene`) in `g_ObjectSpawnFuncs` (0x4647B8) are wrapped, on the map screen 39 and 41 (see World map), and on the title 14 and `Spik Logo` (see Title screen):
- checkpoints are unseen;
- `Spik Deadly` becomes a hazard, `Spik Tree` a Tree, `Spik Stone` a stone (see Grabbing and throwing) and `Spik Ground` an edge of the mud (see Mud);
- `Rope Monkey` is a monkey (object 67, see Monkeys);
- `Mega Dip` dips when landed on, `Mega Jumppad` is a Jumppad, `Mega Throw` is a Throw platform (see Grabbing and throwing), `Mega Steer` the Steer platform, and `Mega Float` platforms ride the tide;
- `Forc Flink` is Flink's Scroll Point;
- `Muta Dummy` is the Forest Dummy, `Muta City` the City dummy, `Muta Moun` the Mountain dummy, `Muta Intr` the Intree dummy, `Muta Worm` the Worm, `Muta Yeti` the Yeti, `Muta Buzz` the Buzzard, `Muta Meta` the Meta dummy, `Muta Storm` the Storm Cloud, `Muta Blitz` the Blitz dude, `Muta Circ` the Circling suckers (the ring and its heads), `Muta Razo` the Razorbacks' spawner and its Razorbacks, `Muta Cycl` the Cyclin' Dude, `Muta Bike` his Chopper Bike, `Muta Litt` the Little Dude, `Muta Fire` the Firemonster and `Muta Holder` the Ingredient Holder, `Muta Thief` the Thief, `Muta Visje` the Visje and `Muta Statue` the Statue;
- `Pots Chest` is a chest;
- `Gene Flink` is Flink's Generator.

See Levels, Platforms, Jumppads, Seesaws, Water, Enemies, Trees, Monkeys, Chests, Hidden objects and Grabbing and throwing. The two calls in Player_Update's contact loop go through stubs (see Enemies), the hint box's draw function stored at 0x433B9E is FLINK.DLL's (see Chests), Game_Frame's call of Player_Update (0x404C74) goes through `player_tick`, and Rope_WaitForGrab's call of Player_TryGrabHangPoint (0x429FE0) through `try_hang` (see Grabbing and throwing), and Actors_DrawAndCull's call that draws the player (0x40F1C0) through `player_draw` (see Mud), and Game_Resume's call of CD_Resume (0x404A88) through `cd_resume` (see Music, Played once), and Player_Update's call of Fx_SpawnGroundDebris (0x410E06) through `land_debris` and the step hook stored at 0x40F95C (see Landing dust).

**Sound.** `Sound_PlayEffect` (0x40D5F0) plays a sound only when its source's plane is drawn at a depth from 0x4D to 0xB0, scaled by depth / 0x90. `Planes_UpdateDepth` gives the planes depths from a table at 0x464B28, `{0x90, 0x40, 0x20, 0x10}` in order, so Lomax's play plane, which comes first, gets 0x90. A Flink map's play plane comes second (plane 0 holds Flink's priority tiles), so it got 0x40 and all of Lomax's sound effects were muted. While a Flink map is loaded, the table is `{0xA0, 0x90, 0x20, 0x10}`. The play plane gets 0x90, and plane 0 stays in front of the play plane's actors, which are drawn 2–14 above their plane. Flink's title screen, whose play plane is second too, gets the same table.

**Powers.** Flink's levels have no use for Lomax's helmet powers, so in Flink's maps they can't be reached (`powers.c`); their code stays as it is, and Lomax's levels keep them:
- Ability uses no power: it picks things up and throws them instead (see Grabbing and throwing). `Level_Start` copies the control scheme's Ability mask into `g_AbilityButtonMask` (0x52C57C), which every power checks. In Flink's maps its load at 0x40FB1E (`mov edx, [esi + 0x464590]`) is `xor edx, edx`, so the mask is 0. The cheat's fly mode reads that mask too (`Player_Update` 0x40FD43: with `g_bCheatsEnabled`, Ability pressed with one of LookDown, Run or LookUp held, and the helicopter chosen, toggles player state 0x20000). In Flink's maps `grab.c` (`fly_cheat`) does the same with the control scheme's Ability button and without the helicopter (no power can be chosen there); that press then neither picks up nor throws.
- Select opens the wheel of Flink's spells instead of the power wheel: `Player_Update`'s call of `PowerWheel_Open` at 0x40FCD6 goes through a wrapper, which calls `spell_wheel_open` (see Spells).
- The HUD shows no power icon or ammo at the top left: `Hud_Draw`'s last four `Actor_Draw` calls (0x42A6C1, 0x42A6EF, 0x42A754, 0x42A773) go through a wrapper; the first draws the magic glass and the spirits held instead (see Spirits), and right of them the chosen spell and its cost (see Spells). Lomax+ moves those sprites' x, not the calls.

All of this acts only on Flink's levels; the bonus level and demos are Lomax's, the map is Lomax's map screen over Flink's world map (see World map) and the title Lomax's title screen over Flink's (see Title screen).

**Source layout.** `main.c` checks every patch site (`retail`), then installs each file's patches; the object spawn wrappers are in its `SPAWNS` table. `game.h` holds what the mod knows of LOMAX.EXE: addresses, the game's functions as typed calls (`Actor_Kill(a)`), and accessors for actor, placement and plane fields (`A_X(a)`, `D_PARAM(def, n)`, `P_W(plane)`). `flink.h` declares what the files share. Each feature has its own file, whose header comment explains its patches: `levels.c` (the table, loading, sound depths), `exits.c`, `music.c`, `checkpoint.c`, `gone.c`, `water.c`, `platforms.c`, `jumppad.c`, `seesaw.c`, `deco.c`, `enemy.c` with `dummy.c`, `firemonster.c`, `holder.c` and `thief.c` (`enemy.h`), `contact.c`, `stomp.c`, `chests.c`, `hints.c`, `generator.c`, `monkey.c`, `powers.c`, `spells.c`, `spellfx.c`, `password.c`, `grab.c`, `groundtype.c`, `dustcloud.c`, `snake.c`, `leaves.c`, `throwplat.c`, `seesaw.c`, `steer.c`, `scroll.c`, `worldmap.c` and `title.c`.
